# G1.1 + G1.2, PR3 — G1.2: the research file, consent and the event log — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** execute this plan task by task, in order, test first. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax.
> No plan-execution skill is installed in this repository, so follow the steps as written. **The tree wins:** where a
> measurement disagrees with this plan, stop, say so, and re-derive; never force the plan's expectation. Read
> [the index](2026-11-03-g11-g12-served-versions-and-event-log.md), [the PR1 plan](2026-11-03-g11-g12-pr1-served-versions-core.md)
> and [the PR2 plan](2026-11-03-g11-g12-pr2-opening-record.md) first: the index's decisions, its test placement and its
> Global Constraints, PR1's "Interfaces produced for later PRs" and PR2's "Handoff to PR3" bind this plan.

**Goal:** every act a consenting editor makes on a chemistry segment, module or edit is written once, synchronously,
to a separate research file (`pipeline-output/research.db`), with the served version it was made on, the editing row
it touched and that row's prior state filtered so another person's data appears only as a hash; a failed write never
fails the act and is counted in a content-free loss ledger, only for consenting editors; nothing is captured for a
decliner, an undecided editor, a visitor or a non-study book; and nothing is captured for anyone until [USER] lists an
approved consent text (G1.2, with G1.4's data side; spec §5.1, §5.9–§5.14, §5.16, §5.20, §5.22).

**Architecture:** one never-throw `sessions.db` migration (predicted `055`) adds `research_consent`,
`research_capture_losses` and `book_switch_events`, the tables that must work while the research file is down. The
research file has its own schema module (`server/lib/researchSchema.js`, never in `server/migrations/`), its own
lazy handle with a 100 ms wait (`server/services/researchDb.js`), and is opened at boot without ever blocking it. One
never-throwing call, `researchLog.record(event)`, applies the study scope, the consent gate, the replay rule and the
filter rule, stamps the flag snapshot and the deployed commit, and writes the event and its items in one
`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction under a random 63-bit `BigInt` id; any failure after the gate is one loss row.
`server/routes/research.js` lets a member record or withdraw their own decision (no page: G1.4 writes the text).
The services that change an editing row return its prior state under additive keys, which the routes strip from
every response in the same commit; one emission module, `server/services/researchEmission.js`, then turns each
route's act into one `record` call after the commit and before the response, including PR1's refusals, the save's
concurrency refusal and the accept's eligibility refusals (`write-refused`, decision 49) and PR2's opening
(`module-opened`). **Capture is inert until an approved text is listed; the
builders are not.** `emit` builds an event before `record`'s gate runs, so its work runs on every emitting request,
for every actor and book: in-memory work, plus at most one module load per save that carries a repetition item (the
claim check, Task 19) and one review-row read per review completion (Task 24). Review Focus 39.

**Tech Stack:** Node 22, Express (CommonJS under `server/`), better-sqlite3 13 (only in `server/node_modules`;
`defaultSafeIntegers` on every research handle), bash (`scripts/record-deployed-commit.sh`), Vitest from the repository
root, Playwright E2E (`cd server && CI=1 npm run test:e2e`). No browser file changes: Task 19's test reads PR1's
`server/public/js/segment-save-state.js` (its exported `ITEM_SOURCES`) from Node.

## Decisions taken in this plan

Each is a §13 item, a seam the spec, the index, PR1's or PR2's handoff leaves to PR3, a reading of the tree, or a
choice between the two halves this plan was drafted in (the research infrastructure, and the emission). **Seven go
beyond the frozen spec's wording, and [USER] approved all seven on 2026-11-04** (spec `✏️ AMENDED 2026-11-04`):
decision 9 (no raw identity in a before-image), decision 26 (the consent POST echoes the text version), decision 48
(apply's per-row outcomes in `detail`), decision 49 (the concurrency and eligibility refusals logged too), decision 54
(propagation stores the client's text once), decision 60 (T11's apply on chemistry with the hold stubbed) and decision
65 (a text identical to another editor's live edit stored as its hash). Decisions 49 and 65 were questions whose "yes"
changed the plan, and are built as rewritten. Decision 16's missing-time half and decision 25 were approved as
written. "Answers ([USER], 2026-11-04)" lists each answer.

**Files, seams and order**

1. **One concern per file; the pure parts in `server/lib/`, tested directly.** `server/lib/researchDbPath.js` (the
   path), `server/lib/researchSchema.js` (the schema steps), `server/lib/researchConsentText.js` (the approved texts),
   `server/lib/researchVocabulary.js` (closed vocabularies), `server/lib/canonicalJson.js`,
   `server/lib/researchFilter.js` (content, before-images, details, items), `server/lib/bookSwitches.js`,
   `server/lib/featureFlags.js`, `server/lib/serverCommit.js`, `server/lib/researchDbHealth.js`; services
   `server/services/researchConsent.js` (the `sessions.db` side: decisions, the capture state, the loss ledger),
   `server/services/researchDb.js` (the `research.db` handle) and `server/services/researchLog.js` (`record`,
   `ensureKey`); route file `server/routes/research.js`; admin script `server/scripts/research-status.js`; shell
   `scripts/record-deployed-commit.sh`. Reason: each has its own failure mode and its own test; `record` composes them.

2. **Test seams, all `_`-prefixed and documented test-only:** `researchDb._setTestDb`, `_setTestPaths`, `_reset`;
   `researchConsent._setTestDb`; `researchConsentText._setTestApprovedTexts`; `serverCommit._setTestServerCommit`;
   `researchLog._reset` (clears the consent-unknown flag). The approved-texts seam is the dangerous one (it can make
   capture start), so Task 4's test fails if any file outside `server/__tests__/` names it. **`researchDb._reset()`
   closes any open handle and returns the state to `unopened`; `_setTestPaths(p | null)` calls `_reset()` first**, so
   the next `getHandle()` opens against the new paths at once instead of returning the handle it already holds (Task
   6 pins it). `serverCommit` is tested through `makeServerCommitReader` on a temporary repository; no test calls the
   production reader, which is rooted at the real tree (spec T13; Task 9).

3. **Task order: the infrastructure, then the services' before-images, then emission route by route, then the
   cross-cutting pins; bent only where a dependency forces it.** The path (Task 1) and the research schema (Task 2)
   come first. The connection's missing-file rule reads `research_consent`, so migration 055 (Task 3), the approved
   texts (Task 4) and the consent service (Task 5) come before the connection and its boot (Task 6); the E2E `rm` line
   lands with the boot, the commit that first makes the server create `e2e-research.db`. `ensureKey` ships with the
   consent routes (Task 7), because §5.14 has the route call it; the route cases that need `record` move to Task 11.
   Then the flags (Task 8), the server commit (Task 9), the vocabularies and filter rule (Task 10), `record` (Tasks 11,
   12), health (Task 13) and `research-status.js` (Task 14). The services' before-images (Tasks 15–18) each strip the
   new keys from every response in the same commit (decision 44), so no commit sends a before-image to a browser.
   Emission goes route by route (Tasks 19–26), and the cross-cutting pins come last (Tasks 27–31), Task 27 after the
   three emitting tasks it drives.

**Identifiers and writes**

4. **Representation of `research_events.id`: a JavaScript `BigInt`, everywhere.** It is a random positive 63-bit
   integer: `BigInt('0x' + crypto.randomBytes(8).toString('hex')) & 0x7fffffffffffffffn`, redrawn if zero. **The
   research handle is opened with `db.defaultSafeIntegers(true)`**, so every integer it reads is a `BigInt`; every test
   and script that reads `research.db` does the same, and no code converts an id to `Number`. Measured 2026-11-03 on
   better-sqlite3 13 (`server/node_modules`): reading `9007199254740993` without safe integers returns the `Number`
   `9007199254740992` **with no error** — a silent wrong value, so this is a by-construction rule, pinned by Task 6's
   test (exact round trip, and the control showing the lossy read). `record` returns `eventId` as a `BigInt`. A
   primary-key collision (probability about n²/2⁶⁴) is not retried; it becomes a `constraint` loss.

5. **`key_seq` is `MAX(key_seq) + 1` for the key, read and written inside one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction**
   (`db.transaction(fn).immediate()`), with `UNIQUE (editor_key, key_seq)`. `IMMEDIATE` takes the write lock first, so
   the read and the insert are atomic against any other connection, and the short busy timeout bounds the wait.

6. **A duplicate is found by a `SELECT` inside that transaction, before the insert**, not by catching a `UNIQUE`
   error. So a real constraint failure stays a `constraint` loss and a replay of a recorded act returns `duplicate`
   (spec §5.11: "a duplicate is not a loss").

7. **`editingContentSha256` is a `record` input** (a plan decision; the index gives this plan `record`'s input to own):
   §5.9 defines `editing_content_sha256` but its input list omits it, and `record` cannot derive it for a reviewer's
   event. `record` validates it (64 lowercase hex or
   null) and looks `base_is_sha256` up itself from `served_text_versions` by `servedVersionId`; a `servedVersionId`
   naming no row fails the build (a `other` loss), since an event whose base cannot be checked by value defeats §5.9's
   purpose.

**Who acted, and what a before-image may keep**

8. **"The actor's own" is `String(row.<identity column>) === String(actorProviderId)`, because every identity column on
   an editing row holds the Entra provider id (the token's `sub`), never `users.id`.** `requireAuth` sets `req.user.id =
   decoded.sub` (`server/middleware/requireAuth.js:52-58`), and `sub` is the Microsoft object id
   (`server/services/auth.js:283-291`, `:337`). The writers stamp it: `editor_id` from
   `server/routes/segment-editor.js:374` and `:428` (`String(req.user.id)`) and `:1510` (propagation: the route passes
   `req.user.id`, and `createPropagatedEdits` stores `String(editorId)`, `server/services/propagationService.js:157`);
   `accepted_by` from `:896`; `reviewer_id` from `:1099`, `:1133`, `:1166`
   (`req.user.id`); `segment_discussions.user_id` from `:1315`; `module_reviews.submitted_by` from `:1013` and
   `reviewed_by` from `:1254`. The columns are `TEXT` (`server/migrations/008-segment-editing.js:32`, `:34`;
   `043-segment-acceptances.js:27`). The `String()` comparison is the precedent of
   `server/services/acceptanceService.js:253`, and every `emitX` passes `actorProviderId: req.user.id` unchanged. A
   provider id can be rebound to a users row by an email match (`server/services/auth.js:221-230`, `:244-246`); a row
   stamped under an earlier provider id then compares unequal, which hashes the text and drops the time: the safe
   direction. The actor is known only in the route (`unapproveEdit(editId)` and `returnEditToPending(editId)` take no
   actor, `segmentEditorService.js:499`, `:543`; `applyApprovedEdits` receives a username, `:842-843`), so every event
   is built there. (Both drafting halves reached this independently.)

9. **Identity is never stored raw in a before-image; the actor's own TIMES are kept.** The field table says identity
   and time are "kept only when they are the actor's own **(the actor's identity is the key, so only their own times
   survive)**", and §6's table gives research events "a random key per consent interval" and no user id. Read
   literally, "kept" would put the actor's own Entra id beside their key in `research.db` and re-identify every event
   of the interval. So `editor_id`, `editor_username`, `reviewer_id`, `reviewer_username`, `accepted_by` and
   `accepted_by_username` are always dropped (the event's key is the actor's identity), and `created_at` /
   `reviewed_at` / `accepted_at` survive only when the matching identity column is the actor's. **✅ Approved by
   [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04).** It resolves a conflict inside the frozen spec, and amends the field
   table that §14 call 14 approved (the table ends *"(Claude's call: this field table.)"*). The fallback, for the
   record, was to store the actor's own identity fields verbatim, against §6's table.

10. **The classes of columns the spec's table does not name** (decided here, pinned by a drift guard in Task 10 that
   fails when a migration adds a column nobody classified):

   | Column | Table | Class | Reason |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `id`, `book`, `chapter`, `module_id`, `segment_id` | both | locator, kept | name no person and no time; the replay rebuild (T11) needs them |
   | `applied_at` | both | replaced by `applied` (boolean) | the spec's "applied (as a boolean)"; whose time it is is not on the row, so "the actor's own" can never be shown and the time is dropped |
   | `review_id` | `segment_edits` | dropped | a link to a module-review row, which carries its submitter; not needed for the rebuild |
   | `displayed_edit_id`, `displayed_edit_sha256` | `segment_edits` | state, kept | part of the base record a re-save moves (§5.8); an id and a hash, never text |
   | `accepted_content` | `segment_acceptances` | base, dropped | the served MT; `served_version_id` identifies it, as for `original_content` |
   | `superseded_at` | `segment_acceptances` | dropped | the time of whoever's save superseded it, not on the row |
   | `superseded_reason` | `segment_acceptances` | state, kept | |

   A column present in a row but absent from the table is dropped at run time (never stored unclassified).

**What `record` does with its input**

11. **The content filter is an allowlist** (`text`, `note`, `comment`, `category`, `version`). `note` and `comment`
   become `{ length, sha256 }` (length in Unicode code points, `sha256Text` from PR1's `server/lib/servedText.js`, the
   one hash function), `null` when absent or `''`. `text` is stored verbatim, except that `record` alone replaces it
   with `textSha256` when it is byte-identical to another editor's live edit (decision 65). Any other key throws
   inside the build, a `textSha256` sent by a caller included, so it becomes a counted `other` loss rather than a
   silent store of something nobody filtered.

12. **Items:** the item vocabulary is closed (§5.10's six types); `outcome` is derived from the event type
   (`edit-save`, `edit-resave` → `applied`; `suggestion-reject` → `rejected`; `suggestion-left-open` → `left-open`);
   items on any other event type, an unknown `itemType`, a `claim` outside `validated | unverified | mismatch`, or an
   unknown item key throw inside the build (an `other` loss). **So the emission (Task 19) validates
   the client's list before calling `record`** and never pass a client-controlled string as `itemType`.

13. **`content_json`, `detail_json`, `before_json`, `flag_snapshot_json` and each item's `detail_json` are canonical
   JSON** (keys sorted at every depth, `server/lib/canonicalJson.js`), so equal content stores equal bytes.
   `before_json` is always an array of `{ table, row }`.

14. **A route-side builder that throws is a counted loss, through `record`: `emit` calls `record({ ...head,
   buildFailed: true })`, and `record` treats the flag, after its gate, as a step-4 failure** — one `other` loss row for
   a consenting actor, nothing for a decliner, an undecided editor or a non-study book, and never an event. Reason:
   §5.11 step 6 counts "a bug" as a loss, but an event built in the route can fail before `record` runs; without the
   flag that bug would be invisible. It adds one input to `record`, whose input the index gives this plan to own, and
   no column. `emit`'s own log carries only `{ errorCode, eventType, book }` (decision 21's shape). Built in Tasks 12
   and 20.

**The gate and the ledger (§5.11)**

15. **The current decision is the user's highest-`id` `research_consent` row**, not the latest `given_at_ms`, so a clock
   step cannot reorder decisions (the rule §5.13 uses for `book_switch_events`).

16. **The replay rule:** a body with `replayed === true` whose `clientActionTime` is missing, or earlier than the
   interval's start (its starting row's `given_at_ms`), is not captured and not counted (§5.11 step 3). A missing time
   cannot prove the act came after consent, so it is treated as before. A live body (`replayed` not true) is never
   judged by its client clock. **The missing-time half goes beyond §5.11's wording** (it exempts only an earlier time)
   and leaves such an act uncounted, against §3's "every loss countable"; it is listed under the disagreements, and
   **✅ [USER] approved the rule as written on 2026-11-04.** Reachability, from PR1's plan: PR1's client always sends
   `clientActionTime` (its "What the client sends"), so only a body queued by a page loaded before PR1's deploy lacks
   it, and `saveRetry` marks such a `seg:` or `acc:` body `replayed` (PR1 decision 27). A save from such a page carries
   no `baseSha256` and is refused `BASE_MISSING` (PR1 decision 28), so what goes uncounted is that refusal's
   `write-refused`, never an edit; an old accept can land. Either is replayed at the first page load after PR1's deploy,
   long before any interval can start (no text is listed until PR4 and G1.4).

17. **A throw in step 2 (`findByProviderId`) or step 3 (the consent read) is a `consent-unknown` loss**, written with no
   interval if the ledger can be written, and it sets the in-memory flag `/api/health` reports; the next successful
   lookup clears the flag. §5.11 names only the consent lookup; a failing identity lookup equally leaves the consent
   state unknown. **One identity failure does not throw and is not covered:** with the `users` table missing,
   `findByProviderId` returns `null` (`server/services/userService.js:57-58`, through the `sqlite_master` probe
   `isUserTableReady`, `:43-53`), so every act reads `skipped / no-user` and writes no loss row (measured on a migrated
   database: `users` renamed away → `null`; `user_book_access` renamed away → `SQLITE_ERROR no such table`, thrown from
   `getBookAccess`, `:158-170`). `checks.schema` names the missing table (PR1 Task 4); Task 12 pins both shapes, and
   Review Focus 40 records the gap.

18. **One loss row per lost act:** when the act has a `client_action_id`, a second loss of the same act for the same
   interval writes nothing (`INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS`). §5.11 dedupes a later *successful* replay in analysis; this
   keeps a repeatedly *failing* replay from counting twice too.

19. **`research_capture_losses` declares no id column** (the spec lists none). Its implicit rowid leaves gaps when a
   withdrawal deletes an interval's rows; that is weaker than what A3 already accepts (whole loss rows surviving in
   the editorial backups), so a random key is not worth its code here.

20. **Error classes:** `RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE` (any reason the handle cannot be had) → `unavailable`; `SQLITE_BUSY*` and
   `SQLITE_LOCKED*` → `busy`; `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT*` → `constraint`; everything else (a `TypeError` from the build,
   `SQLITE_FULL`, `SQLITE_IOERR*`, a corrupt file met mid-write) → `other`.

21. **The failure log is `logger.error({ errorCode, errorClass, eventType, book }, …)` and nothing else** — never
   `err`, never `err.message` (a build `TypeError`'s message could name a field value), never a key, user id or
   provider id (§5.11 step 2). `errorCode` is `err.code || err.name`.

**The research file (§5.1)**

22. **`researchDb` states:** `open`; `unavailable` (re-attempted at most once per `RETRY_INTERVAL_MS` = 60 000, measured
   from the last attempt); and two sticky states that only a restart clears: `corrupt` (`SQLITE_NOTADB`,
   `SQLITE_CORRUPT*`, at open or noted mid-write) and `same-path`. The file is created **only** by
   `fs.closeSync(fs.openSync(p, 'a', 0o600))` and only while `sessions.db` holds no `consented` row; every open uses
   `{ timeout: BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS, fileMustExist: true }`, so SQLite itself never creates it. An existing file and any
   `-wal`/`-shm` are `chmod 0600` before the open. A consent table that cannot be read at that moment leaves the file
   uncreated, reason `consent-unavailable`. `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS = 100` and `RETRY_INTERVAL_MS` are enforceable values
   pinned by Task 6's test. Measured 2026-11-03: with `{ timeout: 100 }` the handle reports `PRAGMA busy_timeout` 100, a
   `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` behind another connection's `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` throws `SQLITE_BUSY` after 102 ms, the `-wal` and
   `-shm` SQLite creates are mode `0600` (umask `022`), and a garbage file throws `SQLITE_NOTADB` at the first pragma.

23. **The research schema is one step today (`user_version` 1).** Foreign keys inside `research.db` (none cross files,
   §5.1): items → events `ON DELETE CASCADE`; `research_events.editor_key` → `editor_keys(editor_key)`, so a key row
   cannot be deleted while its events exist (PR4 deletes the key last, §5.15). CHECKs only on spec-fixed enums
   (`replayed`, `commit_source`, `code_dirty`, `outcome`, `claim`, hash lengths); `event_type` and `item_type` are
   closed in JavaScript (`researchVocabulary.js`), because a CHECK cannot be widened without a table rebuild. **A file
   whose `user_version` is higher than this code knows is unavailable** (a rolled-back deploy must not write into
   columns it does not know).

24. **Boot:** `server/index.js` calls `researchDb.openAtBoot()` right after `failLoudOnMigrationErrors(…)` (Task 6) and
   warms `serverCommit.getServerCommit()` right after that (Task 9), each inside its own try/catch with a comment, so
   the server always boots (§5.1).

**Consent (§5.14)**

25. **PR3 builds the routes and no consent page. ✅ Approved as written by [USER] 2026-11-04.** G1.4 has not chosen
   between in-tool and paper consent, the wording is G1.4's, and while the approved list is empty the page could only
   say there is nothing to agree to. The routes are the data side both choices need; the page is G1.4's, built when its
   text exists. Smaller and reviewable.

26. **`POST /api/research/consent` requires the client to echo the `textVersion` it showed**, and answers **409
   `CONSENT_TEXT_CHANGED`** when it is not the current approved version, so a decision made on an older text is never
   stamped with a newer one (the G1.1 echo principle, applied to consent). **✅ Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec
   AMENDED 2026-11-04):** §5.14 named only "records the caller's own decision against the approved text the server
   serves". The fallback, for the record, was to ignore `textVersion` and record against the current text.

27. **`GET` returns a derived state, `undecided | consented | declined | withdrawn`**, the version the caller decided on
   and the current version, and no time (§11). A withdrawal sets `withdrawn_at_ms` and inserts no row, so the latest
   row stays `consented`; reporting it as `consented` would be false.

28. **The same decision on the same text is `unchanged`** (no new row); a `consented` decision on a new text version
   during a live interval inserts a row carrying the live interval's id (§5.14); only a decision that starts an
   interval calls `ensureKey`.

29. **`POST …/withdraw` with no live interval answers 200 and changes nothing** (a double click is harmless). It
   sets `withdrawn_at_ms` only on the interval's starting row (index decision 5); PR4 adds the deletion.

30. **The consent routes write no `activity_log` row and log no decision** ("nobody routinely sees a decision",
   §5.14); a test compares `activity_log` by value before and after.

31. **`researchConsentText.js`'s last entry is the current text.** "Never removed while a live interval names it" is
   guarded three ways, none of them a production read: the module's header says so and gives the read-only query a
   database administrator runs before removing a version; Task 4's test holds `EVER_LISTED` (every version ever listed,
   appended in the commit that lists it) and fails if any is missing from the list, so a removal is a two-file edit a
   reviewer sees; and `recordDecision` refuses a text that is not listed.

**Flags (§5.13)**

32. **The registry's readers call each consumer's own predicate**, never a copy: `applyHold.faithfulWriteHold`
   (`server/lib/applyHold.js:26`), `pipelineHold` and `figureReviewClosed` (`server/lib/heldBookWrites.js:31`, `:34`),
   `userService.isAssignmentEnforced` (`server/services/userService.js:567`),
   `localizationReviewService.isReviewEnabled` (`server/services/localizationReviewService.js:52`),
   `auth.signInPolicy(process.env.AUTO_CREATE_USERS)` (`server/services/auth.js:180`), `greynirEngine.isEnabled()`
   (`server/services/greynirEngine.js:77`), `notifications.isEmailConfigured()`
   (`server/services/notifications.js:208`), `bookSwitches.currentSwitchValue` and `researchScope.isStudyBook`. Each
   value is a boolean (the sign-in policy a fixed word), never a URL, host or secret. **One entry is added to §5.13's
   list: `not-minted`** (`servedText.isNotMintedBook`), read by every save on the editing path since PR1
   (`saveOnServedBase` step 1). `auth.js` throws at load without `JWT_SECRET` (`server/services/auth.js:28-30`), so its
   reader requires it lazily. A reader that throws fails the snapshot, and so the build: a counted `other` loss (§5.11
   steps 4 and 6).

33. **The env scanner's file list is §5.13's plus the files the registered flags actually read, plus the new modules.**
   Measured 2026-11-03: the files §5.13 names that exist at `ed10180c` hold **no** `process.env` read at all
   (`grep -acoE 'process\.env'` prints 0 for each); the reads behind `greynir` and `email` live in
   `server/services/greynirEngine.js` (`:77`, `:91`) and `server/services/notifications.js` (`:209`, `:227-237`, `:313`,
   `:500`, `:552`, `:670`), both reached from the editing path (`server/services/segmentEditorService.js:332`;
   `server/routes/segment-editor.js:56`). So the list adds those two; `services/moduleOpenings.js` (PR2's handoff: it
   reads no `process.env`, and listing it guards a future read); `featureFlags.js`, `bookSwitches.js`,
   `researchDbPath.js`, `researchDb.js`, `researchConsent.js` and `researchLog.js` (Task 8, all existing by then);
   `serverCommit.js` (Task 9) and `researchEmission.js` (Task 19), each in the commit that creates it. That makes §5.1's
   "no second environment variable" a mechanical check. A listed file that does not exist fails the test (a rename
   cannot drop coverage), and a `process.env` use the scanner cannot name (destructuring, a computed key) fails as
   `<unparsed>`.

34. **`book_switch_events` gets readers, no writer** (`server/lib/bookSwitches.js`: `currentSwitchValue`,
   `firstSwitchOnMs`, `SWITCH_NAMES`); G1.23 builds the route that writes it (§5.13). The snapshot carries the
   current value only, never a switch time (§5.22).

**The server commit (§5.12)**

35. **`deploy.sh` calls a small writer, `scripts/record-deployed-commit.sh FETCH_HEAD pipeline-output/.deployed-commit`,
   right after `git pull --rebase origin main`** (`scripts/deploy.sh:73`) and before the stash is re-applied
   (`:76-85`). It deletes the old file first and writes `{"upstream":"<sha>","head":"<sha>"}` through a temp file and
   `mv`, so a failed write leaves no stale file and the server falls back to `git-head`. A separate script, not inline
   shell, so Task 9's test can run the real writer and the real reader on a temporary repository: one format, two
   languages, one test. `FETCH_HEAD` is exactly what that pull fetched. Its failure prints a warning and never fails the
   deploy (`|| echo …`). No new MIT→AGPL edge: the script touches git only (root `LICENSE` unchanged).

36. **`code_dirty` is `0`, `1` or `NULL`** (`git diff --quiet <commit> -- server tools/lib`: exit 0, exit 1, anything
   else). Not detected, besides §5.12's `server/public/js` edit after boot: an **untracked** new file under `server/`
   (`git diff` does not see untracked files). Recorded in Review Focus.

**Observability, scripts and guards**

37. **`checks.research_db` is `{ ok, reason }` and exactly those two keys**; reasons in precedence
   `consent-unavailable` (the in-memory flag, or `research_consent` unreadable), then `missing` (file absent while a
   `consented` row exists), then `unavailable` (any other probe failure: derived path equal to the editorial one,
   file absent with no consent, open or `PRAGMA user_version` failing, or a version other than
   `RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION`). `backup-stale` is PR4's (index).

38. **`checks.schema` gains PR3's three tables, all columns, and no index** (index decision 3; PR3's indexes serve
   lookups, not correctness).

39. **`research-status.js` reads only `research_capture_losses`** (index decision 6), opens `sessions.db`
   `{ readonly: true, fileMustExist: true }`, and prints per interval the counts by error class and by event type,
   plus the `consent-unknown` total: no day, no module, no text. It never opens `research.db` (reading it is PR4's
   admin reader module's job, §5.16), so §5.1's "every research script opens `research.db` with `fileMustExist`"
   does not bite it.

40. **The write-only surface is pinned:** `Object.keys(require('../services/researchLog'))` is exactly `['_reset',
   'consentLookupFailing', 'ensureKey', 'record']` (§5.16). `ensureKey` returns `{ status }` and never the key.

41. **Append-only (§5.9) is a scanner test**: no `UPDATE`, `REPLACE INTO`, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, upsert (`INSERT … ON
   CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`, the form `server/services/userService.js:587-598` already uses) or `DELETE` on a research
   table anywhere under `server/` outside an allowlist that is empty in PR3 (PR4 adds its two scripts and the withdraw
   deletion). And `activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js` learns `try { researchLog.record(` as a banned pattern, as it bans
   a try-wrapped `activityLog.log(`: `record` cannot throw.

**Emission: the module, the fields, what the services return**

42. **One module, `server/services/researchEmission.js`, and routes call only its `emitX` functions.** It requires
   `researchLog`, `concordanceService`, `servedText`, `researchVocabulary` and `logger`, never `researchDb` or
   `editor_keys` (Task 31's T17 scan reads `server/routes/` for those; this file is under `services/`). `emit(headFn,
   build)` is the one call site of `researchLog.record` here, and **it calls `record` after its `try`/`catch`, never
   inside it**, because `activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js` gains `TRY_WRAPPED_RECORD =
   /try\s*\{\s*researchLog\.record\(/` in Task 11. Reason: a route gains one line per act, the builders are testable
   without HTTP, and nothing a builder does can fail an editor's act.

43. **The research-only fields never refuse and never change a response.** PR1 accepted and ignored them (index decision
   4); PR3 reads them only inside `emitX`, after the act, so a malformed one can only become `null`: `clientActionId`
   kept when a string of 1–128 characters (the bound Task 11's `buildEvent` keeps), `clientActionTime` when a safe
   integer above 0, `replayed` only when `=== true`, `acceptOrigin` when `single` or `keyboard-stream`,
   `reviewedEditSha256` when 64 lowercase hex. T8 requires identical responses whatever the consent state; a 400 for a
   research field would also be a research field failing an act (§3 anti-goal).

44. **One additive key convention, and the strip ships with it.** A service that changes rows returns what it changed,
   read inside the write's transaction, under `before` (raw prior rows, `SELECT *`, which `record` filters; plus the ids
   of the rows it changed where the spec's detail needs them), and propagation returns its per-target facts under
   `writes`. Kept names from PR1's proposal: `saveSegmentEdit`'s `before`, `supersededEditIds`,
   `supersededAcceptanceIds`. New: `submitModuleForReview`'s `stampedEditIds`, `addDiscussionComment`'s `edit`;
   per-target propagation facts go in `writes`, not on PR1's proposed `created` entries, because the propagate route
   sends its result whole. **Routes that put a service result into the response strip these keys, in the service task's
   own commit**, through one route-local helper, `withoutResearchKeys(result)` (a copy minus `before` and `writes`;
   `delete` on a copy, because `eslint.config.js:40` sets no `ignoreRestSiblings` and an unused `const { before, …rest
   }` binding fails `npm run lint` in a commit that does not yet emit). The five review routes and the revoke route send
   the returned row (`segment-editor.js:1113`, `:1147`, `:1180`, `:1198`, `:1234`, `:949`); four routes spread a result
   (`:1663` apply, `:1741` apply-and-render's `applied`, `:1282` review-complete's `applied`, `:1844` restore) or send
   it whole (`:1518` propagate). The emission task then reads `result.before` off the unstripped value. Every such
   route's exact key set is pinned from its service task on (Tasks 16, 17, 18).

45. **Read-modify-write review functions get an `IMMEDIATE` transaction.** `approveEdit`, `rejectEdit`,
   `markForDiscussion`, `unapproveEdit`, `returnEditToPending` (`segmentEditorService.js:401-580`),
   `deleteSegmentEdit` (`:380-392`) and `revokeAcceptance` (`acceptanceService.js:247-272`) read the row and write it
   with no transaction today; the before-image must come from the same transaction as the write (§5.9). `.immediate()`
   takes the write lock first, so the read cannot be followed by a failed upgrade (`SQLITE_BUSY_SNAPSHOT`) that a
   deferred transaction would risk under WAL; better-sqlite3 nests it as a savepoint inside any outer transaction
   (probed on 13.x: an `.immediate()` call inside a running transaction commits with the outer one). The thrown
   refusals (`Edit is not pending`, `PENDING_EXISTS`, `SUPERSEDED_BY_NEWER`) fire before any write, so behaviour is
   unchanged.

46. **`lapseDrifted` keeps returning its count; a new `lapseDriftedIds` returns the ids.**
   `acceptanceService.test.js:502` and `:516` pin the count, and `applyApprovedEdits` reports it as `lapsedAcceptances`
   (`segmentEditorService.js:1083`, `:1121`). `lapseDrifted` becomes `lapseDriftedIds(...).length`, and both run in one
   transaction on the connection given (a savepoint inside apply's). `supersedeForEdit` returns the ids it superseded
   (today `undefined`, `acceptanceService.js:278-287`): additive, since no caller reads its value.

47. **The self-heal reset reads its rows and resets them in one `IMMEDIATE` transaction** (today two autocommit
   `UPDATE`s, `segmentEditorService.js:903-917`), and the rows ride on the recursive call's result as
   `before.selfHealReset = { edits, acceptances }`. The two `UPDATE`s becoming atomic is the only behaviour change. If
   the recursive apply throws (`:918-925`), the reset stays committed, as today, and no `apply` event exists, because
   the act failed (Review Focus 28).

48. **Apply's per-row outcomes go in `detail`, not `content`. ✅ Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec AMENDED
   2026-11-04):** it moves a cell of §5.9's table, which lists "per row: winner applied, losers superseded" under
   Content. Decision 11 fixes `content` to `{ text?,
   note?, comment?, category?, version? }` and `record` throws on any other key (decision 11), so the lists go to
   `detail.applied` and `detail.superseded` (edit ids), with `detail.supersededReviewerNote = { length, sha256 }` of the
   fixed note the apply writes over each loser's (`segmentEditorService.js:1066`, extracted to an exported constant so
   the hash is the code's own string, not a copy). **The acceptances the apply stamps are a per-row outcome too:**
   `applyTransaction` runs `acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn)` (`segmentEditorService.js:1084`),
   which sets `applied_at` on every active, unapplied acceptance (`acceptanceService.js:328-335`), so
   `detail.stampedAcceptanceIds` lists them (read in the apply's transaction just before the stamp, Task 17). Without
   it, an event stream that shows a self-heal resetting an acceptance never shows it applied again, and §8's
   first-completion row (accept, approve, apply) cannot be derived for an acceptance. The fallback, for the record,
   was a `rows` key in `filterContent`'s allowlist (decision 11) holding the three lists.

**Emission: what each event holds**

49. **What logs `write-refused`. ✅ Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04), and widened by that
   answer:** the save's concurrency refusal and the accept's eligibility refusals are logged too. Before the answer
   this decision left both out, for want of a hash to log. Logged, at the save, accept and propagate routes:
   - every caught error carrying PR1's `err.refusal` (every code in `servedEdit.REFUSAL_STATUS`, PR1 Task 13:
     `BOOK_NOT_MINTED`, `NO_LAYER`, `SEGMENT_NOT_FOUND`, `DUPLICATE_ID`, `BASE_MISSING`, `STALE_BASE`,
     `STALE_DISPLAYED`, `STRUCTURE_BLOCKED`), at the save route and the accept route;
   - the save route's `SEGMENT_CONFLICT` (`checkEditConflict`, `segmentEditorService.js:58-75`, which the route runs
     first, `:370`). Its wire answer stays `409 { error: 'conflict', message }` (`segment-editor.js:482-483`; PR1 keeps
     the branch first and unchanged), and **`detail.reason` is the server's code `SEGMENT_CONFLICT`, not the wire's
     `'conflict'`**, because every reason is the thrown `err.code` (for every other refusal the two strings are the
     same);
   - the accept's value check `STALE_CONTENT` (`acceptanceService.js:90-95`), which §5.6 names as the accept's base
     check ("the value check … stays; it is stronger than a hash"), with `echoedSha256 = sha256Text(acceptedContent)`
     and `currentSha256 = sha256Text(placed.seg.is)`;
   - the accept's eligibility refusals, as `acceptSegment` throws them after PR1's placement: `NO_TRANSLATION` (400,
     `:87-89`), `EDIT_EXISTS`, `DISCUSS_OPEN` and `HUMAN_CONTENT` (409, `:96-157`; the route's catch,
     `segment-editor.js:913-929`). They are listed once, as `researchEmission.ACCEPT_ELIGIBILITY_REFUSALS`, and Task 22
     pins that list against the codes `acceptanceService.js` throws, so a new eligibility code cannot go unlogged
     unseen;
   - propagation's organic 409 (PR1 Task 16).

   **Hashes are `null` where the refusal involves none:** `SEGMENT_CONFLICT` compares edit ids and the eligibility
   refusals compare row states, never a text, so both hashes are `null`. The body's `baseSha256` is not taken for
   `SEGMENT_CONFLICT` either (the echo-from-body rule below, Task 21's `ECHO_FROM_BODY`, does not list it): nothing
   compared it. **Not logged:** a 400 the route answers before any service runs (a malformed body), a 404 for a
   missing module, and a 500. `acceptSegment`'s own `SEGMENT_NOT_FOUND` is unreachable behind PR1's placement, which
   refuses first with `err.refusal`. **A decliner's or an undecided editor's refused act is still not logged:** `emitRefused` calls
   `record`, whose consent gate drops it with no event and no loss row (Tasks 21 and 22 pin it at route level).
   The event: `servedVersionId` `NULL` (§5.9's source table), `occurrence` `NULL`, no editing row, no content, no
   items, `segmentId` as the client named it (`null` unless a string of at most 200 characters), the request's
   `clientActionId`, `clientActionTime` and `replayed`, `origin` the write path attempted (`editor-save`, `NULL` for an
   accept, `propagation`), and **`detail = { reason, echoedSha256, currentSha256, action }`**, where `action` is `save`,
   `accept` or `propagate`. **`action` is this plan's addition** to §5.9's detail ("reason code; echoed and current
   hash"), which does not say which write was refused; `detail` is stored unfiltered, so the extra key needs nothing
   from `record`. **Because `detail` is unfiltered, `emitRefused` checks both hashes itself:** PR1 accepts any
   non-empty string as `baseSha256` and any string as `displayedEditSha256` (PR1 decision 14), and PR1 Task 13's
   `refuse('STALE_BASE', { echoedSha256: baseSha256 })` / `refuse('STALE_DISPLAYED', { echoedSha256:
   displayedEditSha256 })` pass them through as sent, so a client string up to the body limit would otherwise land in
   `research.db` (§5.6: "the reason and both hashes, no text"). Each hash is kept only when it is 64 lowercase hex
   (`SHA256_HEX`, Task 19, the check decision 43 applies to `reviewedEditSha256`); otherwise it is `null` and the
   detail gains `echoMalformed: true` (instrument health). **The echoed hash of a save refused before its hash check**
   (`BOOK_NOT_MINTED`, `NO_LAYER`, `SEGMENT_NOT_FOUND`, `DUPLICATE_ID`, and `STRUCTURE_BLOCKED` after it) comes from the
   body's `baseSha256` when it passes the same check, because PR1's `refuse()` carries none for those codes and §5.9's
   detail is "echoed and current hash". An accept body carries no hash (PR1 "What the client sends": `acceptedContent`,
   the value), so an accept refused before its value check has `echoedSha256: null`; only `STALE_CONTENT` hashes the
   value, and the eligibility refusals after it carry no hash either (above).

50. **Claims (§5.10; §14 call 17: check, mark, never refuse).** `claimAssistItems(raw, at)` rebuilds each item from
   known fields only and sets its claim from one table, `CLAIM_VALIDATORS`:
   - **`repetition-insert` is `validated` when `sha256Text(detail.text)` equals `sha256Text` of the `is_text` that
   `concordanceService.suggestionFor(book, chapter, moduleId, segmentId)` returns now**, else `mismatch` (no
   suggestion offered now, or a different one). `suggestionFor` is extracted from `findRepetitions`
   (`server/services/concordanceService.js:238-276`), the function the client's repetition panel reads through
   `GET /repetitions` (`segment-editor.js:1405-1424`), so the panel and the check are one mechanism, including its
   first-copy rule for an id served twice. `unverified` when the sent body carried no text (PR1 decision 36's
   `textDropped`) or when the check itself threw. Every repetition item of one save is checked against the same
   segment, so `claimAssistItems` looks the suggestion up **at most once per call** (a lazy `at.offered()`), not once
   per item: `suggestionFor` loads the module, and the builder runs before `record`'s gate (Review Focus 39).
   The item's `detail.chapter` is the source chapter the client copied from the server's suggestion
   (`rep.suggestion.chapter`, PR1 Task 10), and that value is a **string**: `tm_segments.chapter` is `TEXT`
   (`server/migrations/036-tm-segments.js:26`), `indexModule` writes `String(chapterNum)`
   (`concordanceService.js:111`), and `findRepetitions` passes it through (`:262-270`). So the detail keeps a safe
   integer or a string matching `/^-?\d{1,4}$/`, stored as a `Number` (the events' own `chapter` is an `INTEGER`);
   anything else is `null`.
   - `term-insert`, `markup-wrap` and `draft-restore` are `unverified` (plausibility only; a legacy draft keeps
   `detail.legacy: true`).
   - **`ai-suggestion` and `rule-suggestion` before G1.23: kept and marked `mismatch`, through a seam.** Before G1.23
   no suggestion is offered, so a claim to have accepted one is false, which is what `mismatch` means ("a failed
   check is mismatch"); dropping the item would hide a misbehaving client, and refusing it is ruled out. The two
   table entries are the seam: G1.23 replaces them with its §6 check (spec §5.10, "G1.23 supplies that check").
   The fields G1.23 §8 names (`opId`, `occurrence`, `category`, `runId`, `groupActionId`, `rulingId`, `ruleId`)
   are mapped now, so the seam is the check only.
   - **An item whose `itemType` is not one of §5.10's six is dropped and counted** in the event's
   `detail.droppedAssistCount`: `record` throws on an unknown item type (decision 12), which
   would lose the whole event. A client-sent `claim` is ignored. The server computes `textSha256` and `termSha256`
   (the client never hashes, §5.18), caps an item's text at 10,000 characters (the save route's own cap,
   `segment-editor.js:354`; beyond it `textLength` and `textDropped`), and keeps each item's source only when it
   is one the type allows (`ITEM_SOURCES`, pinned against the client's own table in
   `server/public/js/segment-save-state.js`, PR1 Task 8).

51. **Save events come from `saveOnServedBase`'s `outcome`** (PR1 Task 13): `inserted` → `edit-save`, `updated` →
   `edit-resave` (before: the caller's own prior row), `withdrawn` → `edit-withdraw` (before: the deleted row;
   `servedVersionId` the deleted row's, §5.9's source table), `unchanged` → nothing (nothing was written). Items ride
   only on `edit-save` and `edit-resave` (`record` throws on items elsewhere); a withdraw's assists are not sent.
   `content = { text: editedContent, note: editorNote || null, category: category || null }`, mirroring how
   `saveSegmentEdit` stores them (`segmentEditorService.js:151`, `:196-197`), so a rebuild hashes the same note the row
   holds. `record` stores `textSha256` in place of `text` when the text is another editor's live edit too (decision
   65); the builder passes the text either way.

52. **Reviewer events (`edit-approve`, `edit-reject`, `edit-discuss`):** `editingContentSha256 =
   sha256Text(edit.edited_content)` at the act (a review does not change the text, so before and after agree);
   `content = { note }`; **`detail = { reviewedEditSha256, mismatch }`, where `mismatch = reviewedEditSha256 !==
   editingContentSha256`, so a missing echo is a mismatch** (PR1 decision 35: "when no such row is on screen the
   field is null, and PR3 flags it"). Never refused (§14 call 23). `edit-unapprove` and `edit-return-to-pending`
   carry the before row and nothing else (§5.9).

53. **`clientActionId` goes only on save and accept events and their `write-refused`.** `record` dedupes on
   (`editor_key`, `client_action_id`) across event types (decision 6), so an id on a request that writes several events
   (propagation, review completion and the apply it causes) would collapse them to one. PR1's client sends none there
   anyway ("What the client sends"). A consequence worth knowing: a replay whose first attempt landed now passes the
   displayed check (PR1 decision 39, adopted 2026-11-04) and re-saves the same text, so its `edit-resave` carries the
   landed act's `clientActionId` and is a duplicate, with no second event; if the row moved on to a third text since,
   the replay is still refused `STALE_DISPLAYED` (PR1 Review Focus 18's control), and that `write-refused` carries the
   landed act's id too, so it is a duplicate as well and writes nothing (Task 21). Reviewer events, delete, revoke and
   comment read no client fields either (`NO_CLIENT`, Task 20;
   submit, apply, review completion, restore and propagation use `NONE`): PR1's client sends none there, and a forged
   one would otherwise enter the dedupe.

54. **Propagation: one event per written target, the client's text stored once. ✅ Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04
   (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04):** it changes a cell of §5.9's table, which lists "the client's text" under Detail and
   "the text written" under Content. They are one string by construction (`createPropagatedEdits` writes
   `propagatedText: editedContent`, `segment-editor.js:1507-1516`), so `content.text` holds it (or, under decision 65,
   `content.textSha256`) and `detail.clientTextSha256` its hash, instead of the text twice on every target's event.
   The fallback, for the record, was `clientText` in the detail too. The rest of the detail:
   `previewSha256` (of `latestEditedText ?? is`, read just before the write by a new
   `propagationService.latestLiveEdit`, which `latestEditedText` then delegates to,
   `propagationService.js:173-182`), `previewSource` (`own-edit` when that latest live edit's `editor_id` is the
   actor's, `other-edit` when another editor's, `base` when there is none), `previewText` **only** for `own-edit` or
   `base` (and `record` drops it when another editor holds the same text as a live edit, decision 65),
   `previewMismatch`, `supersededAcceptanceIds`, `source: { moduleId, segmentId }`, `batch: { index, size }`,
   and `skipped` on index 0 only (so a sum over events counts each skip once). "The target's base" is the event's
   `served_version_id` and `base_is_sha256` (`record` looks the hash up), not a repeated field. **A propagation that
   writes nothing writes no event** (nothing changed; Review Focus 27).

55. **`module-opened` uses PR2's seam, by the names PR2's plan fixes** (its decision 11 and its "Handoff to PR3",
   checked against that plan): after `moduleOpenings.openOnServe(conn, …)` returns `{ status: 'opened', opening }`,
   `emitModuleOpened(req, { detail: moduleOpenings.moduleOpenedDetail(served, step.opening) })`, i.e. `{
   servedVersionIds, duplicatedIds, inflightCount }` (spec §5.5 "after commit"). `servedVersionId` `NULL` (§5.9's source
   table). PR2's handoff forbids `record` from ever turning a serve into a 503 or a 503 into a 200; `emit` cannot throw,
   so it cannot.

56. **Review completion and the apply it causes are two events, linked by `reviewId` in both details**, not by a
   research event id: routes ignore `record`'s result (§5.11 step 3), so no route holds an event id to point at.
   §5.9's `review-complete` row gives its detail as "the `apply` event it caused"; the `apply` event with `trigger
   'review-complete'` and the same `reviewId` is that event, found by value (listed under the disagreements).
   `review-complete`: `content = { note: notes }`, `detail = { reviewId, status, apply }` with `apply` one of
   `applied`, `held`, `failed`, `not-attempted`. `apply`: `detail.trigger` (`apply`, `apply-and-render` or
   `review-complete`) and, for the last, `reviewId`. The location comes from the review row
   (`getModuleReviewWithEdits(reviewId).review`, `segmentEditorService.js:682-697`): `completeModuleReview` returns no
   location (`:763-767`), which is also why the route's own activity row reads an undefined `result.book`
   (`segment-editor.js:1287-1289`, a pre-existing defect this plan does not touch; Review Focus 34).

57. **Emission sits after the service returns and before `res.json`**, which moves it ahead of the routes that answer
   first today (revoke `:949`, delete `:982`, submit `:1017`, unapprove `:1198`, review-complete `:1282`, comment
   `:1324`); their `activityLog.log` calls stay where they are. In apply-and-render it sits right after
   `applyApprovedEdits` (`:1713-1718`), before `runPipeline` (`:1721`), so an apply whose pipeline start throws is
   still recorded: the apply committed.

58. **Where the spec's event tables leave a field to the plan:** `chapter` comes from the edit, acceptance or review
   row for routes keyed by an id (it is an `INTEGER`, `-1` for appendices); `occurrence` is the row's (`NULL` on a
   pre-G1.1 row) or the placement's for a save; `editingKind` is `segment_edit` or `segment_acceptance`; acceptance
   events have `origin` `NULL` (acceptance rows carry none, §5.8); `sessionId` is `NULL` until G1.3.

**Tests**

   `supertest` is used by one suite only (`server/__tests__/books-routes.test.js`) and would drag in the middleware
   the handler idiom skips on purpose. Consent states are set through Task 11's fixture
   (`server/__tests__/helpers/researchFixture.cjs`, Task 11: `APPROVED`, `makeUser`, `consent`, `decline`,
   `readResearch`, `losses`) and service (`researchConsent.withdraw`, Task 5); Task 21 adds
   `server/__tests__/helpers/researchEventsFixture.cjs` for reading events back (Task 21). **Every test that applies or

   `pipelineStatusService.transitionStage`** (the idiom of `acceptanceApply.test.js:8-13`, `:159-160`, `:201`): the

60. **T11's apply runs on chemistry in a temp books root with `applyHold.assertFaithfulWritable` stubbed for that
   test. ✅ Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04);** §10 T11 says "on a non-held fixture book".
   Chemistry is the only study book (`researchScope.STUDY_BOOKS`, PR1 Task 2) **and** the only held book
   (`server/lib/applyHold.js:14-18`), and
   `record` drops every non-study book at step 1 (§5.11), so on a non-held book the apply event T11 rebuilds from

   `applyHold.assertFaithfulWritable(book)` through the module object, `:841`) and touches nothing outside the temp

61. **T8 at route level runs the same act script once per consent state, each by its own editor on its own module**,
   and compares the four scripts' responses and editing rows after replacing the scenario's identifiers (provider
   id, username, module id, row ids by order of first appearance) with tokens and masking the second-resolution
   times. The record-level parts of T8 (the file down, no approved text, a replay before consent, a dropped consent

62. **The route-level T10 and T12 (Task 27) come after the tasks that emit `module-opened`, `edit-save` and

   through the routes (index Review Focus 7), which Task 12 covers only below the route.

63. **Tasks 28, 29 and 30 are pins over the emitting tasks, so their first run passes** (the precedent is PR1 decision
   18, its Task 11). Each carries its red arm in the same file (a positive control that can fail: the byte scan that
   does see stored text, the dropped event that does change the rebuild, the planted difference the comparison does

**Strings**

64. **Icelandic strings, ✅ approved as proposed by [USER] 2026-11-04** (index decision 13), in
   `server/routes/research.js` (`CONSENT_MESSAGES`), and nowhere else (the emission tasks add no refusal, notice or
   message). The approval pass changed and added none; a string changed later goes back to [USER] before the PR opens:

   | Code | HTTP | Message (approved as proposed) |
   |---|---|---|
   | `visitor` | 403 | *Gestir eru ekki skráðir notendur og geta ekki tekið afstöðu til rannsóknarinnar; ekkert er geymt fyrir gest.* (body `{ error: 'Visitor', message, visitor: true }`, the shape of `server/routes/notifications.js:137-141`) |
   | `NO_APPROVED_TEXT` | 409 | *Samþykkistexti rannsóknarinnar hefur ekki verið birtur enn, svo ekki er hægt að skrá ákvörðun.* |
   | `CONSENT_TEXT_CHANGED` | 409 | *Samþykkistextinn hefur breyst frá því að síðan var hlaðin. Lesið nýja textann og takið afstöðu aftur.* (decision 26) |
   | `INVALID_DECISION` | 400 | *Ákvörðun verður að vera „consented“ eða „declined“.* |
   | a thrown error | 500 | *Ekki tókst að skrá ákvörðunina.* |

   Task 7 adds three route lines, so `docs/_generated/routes.md` is regenerated in that commit; every later route task
   touches `server/routes/**` without adding a route, so `npm run docs:check` runs at the tip, expected clean.

**Another editor's text (adopted 2026-11-04)**

65. **A saved or propagated text byte-identical to another editor's live edit is stored only as its sha256. ✅
   Approved by [USER] 2026-11-04 (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04).** This was an open question, and the plan built the spec's
   literal rule (the text an act wrote is the actor's, so `content.text` kept it). R1 says another person's data
   reaches research only as a hash, and identical bytes are that data. The case is a pane opened over another editor's
   in-flight edit (PR1 Review Focus 16: the repetition button, a draft restore, or a direct post) and an unchanged
   send, or a propagation of another editor's live edit.
   - **Live**, exactly as spec §5.9 and `propagationService.latestEditedText` (`:173-182`) define it: a `segment_edits`
     row whose `status` is not `rejected` or `superseded` (so `pending`, `discuss` and `approved`, applied or not).
     **Another editor's:** its `editor_id` is not the actor's, by decision 8's `String()` comparison; a row with no
     `editor_id` counts as another's.
   - **On which segments:** the event's own segment, and for `propagation` also the source segment its
     `detail.source` names (decision 54), both in the event's book. An event that names no segment (apply, restore,
     review completion, submit, `module-opened`) checks nothing.
   - **Where the comparison is made, and from which rows:** in `record`'s build (§5.11 step 4, after the consent gate;
     Task 11), by `othersLiveTexts(ev)`: one `SELECT editor_id, edited_content FROM segment_edits WHERE book = ? AND
     module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND status NOT IN ('rejected', 'superseded')` per named segment, on
     `getSessionsDb()`, the connection `record` already reads `served_text_versions` from. It runs after the act
     committed and before the response. The handler runs synchronously from the write to `emit`, and neither a save nor
     a propagation changes another editor's row (`saveSegmentEdit` supersedes only the caller's own `discuss` and
     `rejected` rows, `segmentEditorService.js:138-142`, `:175-179`; `createPropagatedEdits` updates only the caller's
     own pending row, `propagationService.js:139-145`, at `ed10180c`), so the rows read are the rows the act met. An
     identical edit someone saves later does not reach back into an event already written: the first writer keeps
     their text.
   - **What is replaced, with the set those rows give (`withheld`, Task 10):** `content.text` becomes
     `content.textSha256` (`filterContent`); a before-image's own `edited_content` becomes `edited_content_sha256`
     (`filterBefore`: a re-save's or an in-place propagation's prior own text can be that earlier copy, and keeping it
     would undo the earlier event's hash at the next re-save); propagation's `detail.previewText` is dropped
     (`filterDetail`; `detail.previewSha256` already holds its hash). `editing_content_sha256` is unchanged and equals
     `textSha256`, so T11's rebuild resolves the text from the event that carries it: the other editor's own save, if
     they consented (Task 29). Byte-identical is JavaScript string equality on the stored strings, with no normalisation, as
     `sha256Text`.
   - **Why in `record` and not in the builders:** no caller can forget it (the reason `filterBefore` lives there,
     Task 10); a later emitter (re-attach, § Handoff to PR4; G1.23's events) gets it unasked; and it runs after the
     gate, so it adds no read to a decliner's, an undecided editor's, a visitor's or a non-study request (Review Focus
     39 is unchanged). A failed read is a build failure, so a counted `other` loss for a consenting actor (Task 12).
   - **Not covered, by the answer's wording ("a saved or propagated text"):** an assist item's whole text (§5.10: what
     a repetition insert or a draft restore put in place, never the saved text; a repetition text is a published
     translation), and an own text in a before-image of an event that names no segment (apply's losers, the
     self-heal reset; reached only when the applier wrote the row). Review Focus 44.

### Where the tree, the spec, the index and PR1 disagree (recorded; the tree wins)

None of these changes what the frozen spec says beyond the seven decisions above that [USER] approved on 2026-11-04
(9, 26, 48, 49, 54, 60, 65; spec `✏️ AMENDED 2026-11-04`).

- **Spec §5.9's `record` input list omits `editingContentSha256`**, though the column exists and `record` cannot
  derive it for a reviewer's event → decision 7.
- **Spec §5.9's field table** says identity and time are "kept only when they are the actor's own", while its own
  parenthetical and §6's table give events only "a random key per consent interval" → decision 9 (approved). It also
  lists `applied_at` under that rule, which can never be met because the row does not say whose time it is → decision
  10 (the boolean only).
- **Spec §5.9 puts apply's per-row outcomes under Content** and **propagation's client text under Detail and its
  written text under Content** (one string by construction, `segment-editor.js:1507-1516`) → decisions 48, 54
  (approved).
- **Spec §5.9's `write-refused` row covers §5.6 and §5.7, the G1.1 base checks**; [USER]'s 2026-11-04 answer also
  logs the save's `SEGMENT_CONFLICT` and the accept's eligibility refusals, with no hash → decision 49 (approved).
- **Spec §5.9's content rule stores "the text written" as the actor's**; R1 allows another person's data only as a
  hash, and a text byte-identical to another editor's live edit is that data → decision 65 (approved: its hash only).
- **Spec §5.11 step 6 counts "a bug" as a loss** but has no path for an event that fails to build before `record` runs →
  decision 14. It names only the consent lookup for `consent-unknown`, not the identity lookup → decision 17 (and the
  one identity failure that does not throw, a missing `users` table, stays uncounted: decision 17, Review Focus 40);
  and it dedupes a later successful replay but says nothing of a repeatedly failing one → decision 18.
- **Spec §5.11 step 3 exempts only "a replayed act whose client time is earlier than the interval's start"**; decision
  16 also drops a replay with **no** client time, with no event and no loss row, which leaves that act uncounted
  against §3's "every loss countable". Reachable only from a body queued before PR1's deploy (decision 16); [USER]
  approved decision 16's rule as written on 2026-11-04.
- **Spec §5.9's `review-complete` row gives Detail as "the `apply` event it caused"**; no route holds a research event
  id, so both events carry `reviewId` and the link is by value → decision 56.
- **Spec §5.9 says "Re-attach emits through the same call, with the edit's author as actor and origin `re-attach`"**,
  and its `edit-save` row covers "or re-attach inserts one". No path in this project can emit it: the id-based script
  refuses the study book (index decision 7; PR1 Task 17), and re-attach by content (10-10 plan 4.2) is unbuilt (index
  "Not in this plan"). Nothing is built here; § Handoff to PR4 records what its builder must do.
- **Spec §5.13's env-scan file list holds no `process.env` read at all** (`grep -acoE 'process\.env'` prints 0 for each
  of its files that exist at `ed10180c`); the registered flags' reads live in `greynirEngine.js` and `notifications.js`
  → decision 33. §5.13 cites `applyHold.js:43` and `heldBookWrites.js:57-62` for the predicates; those lines are the
  export blocks, and the functions are at `applyHold.js:26` and `heldBookWrites.js:31`, `:34` (substance holds).
- **Spec §5.14 says "the current decision is the latest row"**, but a withdrawal inserts no row, so the latest row of a
  withdrawn editor still reads `consented` → decision 27 (`GET` reports a derived state).
- **Spec §5.1 and T10: "`researchDb` refuses to open when the derived path equals `resolveDbPath()`".** The derivation
  can never return its input, so the guard is reachable only through a test seam → kept as defence in depth (Task 6).
- **Spec §10 T11 says "apply with self-heal (on a non-held fixture book)"**, but the only study book is also the only
  held book, and `record` drops every non-study book → decision 60 (approved).
- **Spec §5.12 cites `scripts/export-segment-edits.js:43-52`** for the never-throw git call; the handoff's `:42-51` is
  one line early. The spec is right.
- **The index's test placement gives T10 and T12 to PR3 whole**; their route-level arms need PR2's opening and this
  plan's emission, so they come last (Task 27).
- **PR1 Task 12 pins a legacy insert's keys as `['id', 'updated']`** → Task 15 updates the pin on purpose.
- **Handoff §4 PR3 places the API 404 handler at `server/index.js:374`**; that is its comment, and the handler is
  `app.use('/api/*path'` at `:375`.
- **Route handler ranges in this plan's first drafts were off by up to four lines at the end**; re-measured:
  submit `:1001-1036`, review-complete `:1246-1296`, comment `:1306-1346`, apply `:1636-1677`, apply-and-render
  `:1684-1757`, restore `:1818-1854`.
- **Pre-existing, outside the spec:** review completion's activity row reads `result.book` / `result.module_id`
  (`segment-editor.js:1287-1289`), which `completeModuleReview` never returns (`segmentEditorService.js:763-767`). Not
  changed here (decision 56).

## Global Constraints (PR3)

The index's Global Constraints bind this PR: test first, one commit per task; the gate is what CI runs (`npm test` from
the repository root, the failing set diffed by name against `main`'s; `npm run lint` **and** `npm run format:check`;
`npm run docs:check`, which applies (Task 7 adds routes, and many tasks touch `server/routes/**`); `cd server && CI=1
npm run test:e2e`, which applies (routes change)); no dependency is added, so
`npm audit` is not part of this gate; module systems; better-sqlite3 only under `server/`; paths from `__dirname` or
`resolveDbPath()`; never-throw schema steps; organic never; no research field in a response; `process.exitCode` in the
new script; `grep -a`; a mutation check restores from a golden `cp`; nothing names a grant or fund; no count, test
total or CI verdict in prose. In addition:

- **No test writes under `books/` or to `pipeline-output/`.** Every test that requires a server module sets
  `SESSIONS_DB_PATH` in a `mkdtempSync` directory first, so the research file derives into it too (Task 1), and every
  route test sets both `segmentParser._setTestBooksDir` and `segmentEditorService._setTestBooksDir` to a temp root (the
  MT lock and the faithful file are written there). Run `git status --porcelain` after every test run.
- **No test runs git on the real tree.** Every test in which `record` can reach its build sets
  `serverCommit._setTestServerCommit(…)` in its setup, so the production reader (rooted at the repository) is never
  called; `serverCommit`'s own tests use `makeServerCommitReader` on a temporary repository (spec T13; Task 9).
- **Research ids are `BigInt`.** Every handle on `research.db` (the service's, the tests', a later script's) is opened
  with `defaultSafeIntegers(true)`; `editing_id` and `served_version_id` are `sessions.db` ids and are compared as
  `BigInt(sessionsId)`; no research event id is ever converted to `Number` (decision 4).
- **`researchLog.record` is called bare** (never inside a `try`; `activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js` bans it from Task 11
  on), synchronously, after the editing write committed and before the response, and its result is never read by a
  route. Routes call only `researchEmission`'s `emitX` functions; no route requires `researchDb` or names
  `editor_keys` (Task 31).
- **The consent gate is inert until [USER] lists a text** in `server/lib/researchConsentText.js`; no task lists one.
  Tests list texts only through `_setTestApprovedTexts`, which no file outside `server/__tests__/` may name (Task 4).
- **Format:** `npx prettier --write` on each `.js` file a task creates or edits under `server/` before its commit.
- **The order is load-bearing** (decision 3): Tasks 1–14 build the infrastructure in dependency order; each service
  task (15–18) strips its new keys from the responses in its own commit; the emitting tasks (19–26) need Tasks 11–12;
  Tasks 27–30 need every emitting task; Task 31 needs Tasks 11–14.

## Review Focus

Input cases the spec implies but its tests do not exercise directly. Each names the task that answers it.

1. **A research write behind another connection's lock** (PR4's `VACUUM`; here a test's `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`) must fail in
   about `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS`, never better-sqlite3's synchronous 5000 ms default, which would stall every request. → Task 6, Task 12
2. **The research file missing while a `consented` row exists** must never be recreated, at boot or from `record`, or
   every editor is silently re-keyed. → Task 6, Task 12
3. **A decliner's or undecided editor's act with the research file down** writes neither an event nor a loss row
   (index Review Focus 7). → Task 12, Task 27
4. **A replay of an act made before consent, or before a re-consent's new interval**, or a replay with no client time:
   not captured, not counted. → Task 11
5. **A withdrawn interval whose latest row is still `consented`** captures nothing, and `GET` reads `withdrawn`. → Task 5, Task 7
6. **A text version removed from the approved list while an interval names it** stops capture without a loss. Only the
   append-only test and the administrator's query stand in the way. → Task 4
7. **An id above 2⁵³** read without safe integers comes back wrong with no error. → Task 6, Task 11
8. **Another person's data in a before-image:** text hashed, free text reduced, identity never raw, another's times
   dropped, unclassified columns dropped. → Task 10
9. **`research_consent` unreadable** (a migration that failed to create it while saves still work): a `consent-unknown`
   loss with no interval, and `checks.research_db` reads `consent-unavailable`. → Task 12, Task 13
10. **The server commit:** a restart without `deploy.sh` reads `git-head`; a manual pull or edit under `server/` after a
    deploy sets `code_dirty`; a `books/`-only change does not; an untracked new file under `server/` is NOT seen
    (decision 36). → Task 9
11. **A flag's secret value** (`GREYNIR_URL`, `SMTP_PASS`) must never reach `flag_snapshot_json`. → Task 8
12. **The request log** records that someone posted `/api/research/consent/withdraw`, and when (path and time, from
    `server/middleware/requestTiming.js`), though not who. It is an editing record already named under R3 (§5.21,
    §6 item 14); not changed here. [USER] answered 2026-11-04 that the consent text must say so; that text is G1.4's
    (a dated note under G1.4 in the Skerfur plan), and the follow-up's status lives in the register. → Task 7
13. **`/api/health` is unauthenticated** (`server/index.js:245`): `checks.research_db` carries no count and no extra key. → Task 13
14. **A route that someday requires `researchDb` or names `editor_keys`.** → Task 31
15. **`record`'s failure log** leaking content, a note, a key or a user id into journald, beyond the deletion promise. → Task 31
16. **A replay whose first attempt was recorded** returns `duplicate` and writes no loss; **a repeatedly failing replay**
    is one loss row. → Task 11, Task 5
17. **`EVENT_TYPES` drift** between the vocabulary (Task 10), the emitters (Tasks 19–26) and G1.23. → Task 10
18. **E2E's boot creates `e2e-research.db`**; the `rm` line must remove it with its `-wal` and `-shm`, from the same
    derivation the server uses. → Task 6
19. **A stale `.deployed-commit`** left by a failed write would stamp the wrong commit; the writer deletes first. → Task 9
20. **A newer research schema than the running code** (a rolled-back deploy): unavailable, never a write into unknown
    columns. → Task 2, Task 6
21. **A builder that throws** (a bug, an unexpected shape) never fails the act, never changes the response, and is
   counted for a consenting actor (decision 14). → Task 20, Task 21
22. **A refused save carries the editor's typed text in its body.** The `write-refused` event must hold no text, no
   note and no items, and the research file's bytes must not contain that text (T4). → Task 21
23. **A replay of an act already recorded** (same `clientActionId`) writes nothing new: one whose first attempt
   landed passes the displayed check (PR1 decision 39) and its `edit-resave` is a duplicate, and one refused because the
   row moved on since is a duplicate too (decision 53). → Task 21
24. **A response that spreads a service result** (apply, apply-and-render, review completion, restore, propagate) or
   returns its row (the review routes, revoke) must stay key-for-key what it was: `before` and `writes` never reach a
   browser, in any commit, not only at the tip. → Task 16, Task 17, Task 18 (the strip and its pins), Task 23,
   Task 24, Task 25 (the same pins, kept)
25. **A reviewer acts on text that changed after the page loaded** (the edit keeps its id through a re-save): the act
   goes through, the event carries both hashes and `mismatch: true`; a missing echo is a mismatch too. → Task 23
26. **A propagation whose preview text is another editor's live edit** stores only its hash; the propagator's own edit
   and the base keep their text, unless another editor holds that same text as a live edit (decision 65). → Task 25,
   Task 28
27. **A propagation that writes no target** (all skipped) writes no `sessions.db` row and no event; a skipped duplicate
   id is listed once, on index 0 of the batch that did write. → Task 25
28. **A self-heal whose recursive apply throws** leaves the reset committed (today's behaviour) and no `apply` event:
   the act failed and answered an error. → Task 17
29. **A repetition suggestion that changed between the insert and the save** (an apply in between indexed a newer
   translation) reads `mismatch` though the client was honest: the check is "the suggestion the server would offer
   now" (§5.10), by design. → Task 19
30. **A term inserted into another segment's textarea** (`server/public/js/segment-editor.js:3261-3263`) is attributed by the
    client (PR1 Task 10); the server cannot check it, so it is `unverified`, never `validated`. → Task 19
31. **A forged `ai-suggestion` or `rule-suggestion` item before G1.23** is kept and marked `mismatch`, never dropped
    and never refused. → Task 19
32. **An unknown item type in a save's `assists`** must not make `record` lose the whole event: it is dropped and
    counted. → Task 19, Task 21
33. **A withdraw whose body still carries assists** sends no items (`record` throws on items outside the save events).
    → Task 21
34. **Review completion's location** comes from the review row; the route's existing activity row reads
    `result.book`, which `completeModuleReview` never returns (pre-existing, not changed here). → Task 24
35. **A request that writes several events** (propagation, review completion plus its apply) carries no
    `clientActionId`, or `record`'s dedupe would keep one; a reviewer's act reads none either. → Task 23, Task 24,
    Task 25
36. **The review functions now take the write lock before they read** (decision 45): under contention they wait up to
    the connection's busy timeout instead of failing an upgrade; the refusals still throw before any write. → Task 16
37. **A decliner's or an undecided or a withdrawn editor's act** leaves the response and every editing row exactly as
    a consenting editor's does, and writes no research row and no loss row. → Task 30
38. **An organic act** (save, accept, propagate) emits a `write-refused` that `record` drops at step 1, with no loss
    row. → Task 22, Task 25
39. **The builders run before the gate**, so their cost lands on every emitting request, for decliners, visitors,
    non-study books and everyone while no text is listed: at most one module load per save that carries a repetition
    item (the claim check, memoized per call) and one review-row read per review completion (`emitReviewComplete`'s
    head, `getModuleReviewWithEdits`, which also selects the module's edits, `segmentEditorService.js:682-697`). A
    scope pre-check in `emit` was not taken: `record` holds the gate (decision 42), and Tasks 22 and 25 pin that an
    organic refusal still reaches `record`. → Task 19, Task 24
40. **A missing `users` table** makes every act `skipped / no-user` with no loss row (decision 17's uncovered case);
    a missing `user_book_access` throws and is `consent-unknown`. → Task 12
41. **A client-chosen string in a refusal's echoed hash** (`baseSha256`, `displayedEditSha256` are type-checked, never
    format-checked, by PR1) must not reach `research.db`: it is `null` with `echoMalformed: true`. → Task 21
42. **An event whose own properties throw when read** (a getter, a proxy) must not make `record` throw from its catch,
    which reads the same fields to log the loss. → Task 11, Task 12
43. **The refusals [USER]'s 2026-11-04 answer adds** (decision 49): the save's `SEGMENT_CONFLICT` answers the wire's
    `{ error: 'conflict' }` unchanged and logs `reason 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT'`; each accept eligibility refusal logs its
    own code; both carry `null` hashes and no text. A request that succeeds logs no `write-refused`, and a decliner's
    refused act logs nothing and counts nothing. A new eligibility code in `acceptanceService.js` fails Task 22's pin.
    → Task 21, Task 22
44. **A text another editor holds as a live edit** (decision 65): a save or propagation that sends it stores
    `textSha256`, never the text, and a later before-image of the actor's own copy stores its hash too; a different
    text keeps its text; a `rejected` or `superseded` row, or the actor's own row, holding the same text withholds
    nothing; and a decliner's copied text is absent from the research file's bytes. **The boundary:** an assist item's
    whole text, and an own text in the before-image of an event that names no segment (apply), are not checked. →
    Task 10, Task 11, Task 21, Task 25, Task 28, Task 29

### Task 1: the research file's path, derived from `sessions.db`'s

Spec §5.1 (Path), T10 (the path half); decision 3 (the E2E `rm` line lands with the boot, Task 6).

- Test: `server/__tests__/researchDbPath.test.js` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `resolveDbPath()` (`server/lib/dbPath.js:10-15`).
- Produces: `deriveResearchPath(p: string): string` — the same directory; in the base name only, every `sessions` →
  `research`; a base name with no `sessions` gets `.research` before its extension (or at its end when it has none).
  `resolveResearchDbPath(): { researchPath: string, editorialPath: string }`, read at call time, never cached.

```js
import { describe, it, expect, afterEach } from 'vitest';
import { createRequire } from 'module';
const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const os = require('os');

const SERVER = path.join(__dirname, '..');
const ORIG = process.env.SESSIONS_DB_PATH;
afterEach(() => {
  if (ORIG === undefined) delete process.env.SESSIONS_DB_PATH;
  else process.env.SESSIONS_DB_PATH = ORIG;
});

describe('deriveResearchPath (spec §5.1; T10, path half)', () => {
  const { deriveResearchPath } = require('../lib/researchDbPath');
  it('maps every shape the suite and production use', () => {
    const cases = [
      ['/x/pipeline-output/sessions.db', '/x/pipeline-output/research.db'],
      ['/x/pipeline-output/e2e-sessions.db', '/x/pipeline-output/e2e-research.db'],
      ['/tmp/acc-test-1.db', '/tmp/acc-test-1.research.db'], // spec T10's example
      ['/tmp/acc-test-4242.db', '/tmp/acc-test-4242.research.db'],
      ['/tmp/acc-apply-4242.db', '/tmp/acc-apply-4242.research.db'],
      ['/w/authz-test.db', '/w/authz-test.research.db'],
      ['/w/sessions/acc.db', '/w/sessions/acc.research.db'], // a directory named sessions is untouched
      ['/w/noext', '/w/noext.research'],
    ];
    expect(cases.map(([p]) => deriveResearchPath(p))).toEqual(cases.map(([, r]) => r));
  });
  it('never returns its input (so a research file cannot be the editorial one)', () => {
    for (const p of ['/a/sessions.db', '/a/b.db', '/a/research.db', '/a/x']) {
      expect(deriveResearchPath(p)).not.toBe(p);
    }
  });
  it('the real suites still use the names above (re-point this test if one is renamed)', () => {
    const uses = [
      ['__tests__/acceptanceService.test.js', 'acc-test-${process.pid}.db'],
      ['__tests__/acceptanceApply.test.js', 'acc-apply-${process.pid}.db'],
```
