```js
      ['e2e/playwright.config.js', "'e2e-sessions.db'"],
    ];
    expect(
      uses.filter(([f, s]) => !fs.readFileSync(path.join(SERVER, f), 'utf8').includes(s))
    ).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('resolveResearchDbPath', () => {
  it('follows SESSIONS_DB_PATH at call time, with no second variable', () => {
    const { resolveResearchDbPath } = require('../lib/researchDbPath');
    process.env.SESSIONS_DB_PATH = '/tmp/acc-test-7.db';
    expect(resolveResearchDbPath()).toEqual({
      researchPath: '/tmp/acc-test-7.research.db',
      editorialPath: '/tmp/acc-test-7.db',
    });

    expect(researchPath.endsWith(path.join('pipeline-output', 'research.db'))).toBe(true);
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it**

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchDbPath.test.js`
Expected: FAIL — `Cannot find module '../lib/researchDbPath'`, reported against the first `describe` (its `require`
runs while the suite is collected, before any `it` is registered, so none of its three cases runs) and against the
`resolveResearchDbPath` case.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`server/lib/researchDbPath.js` (header: spec §5.1; why `sessions` → `research` in the base name only, and `.research`
otherwise — several suites set names with no `sessions`, and a plain replace would point the research file at the
editorial test database; why there is no second environment variable — the server loads `.env`,
`server/index.js:24`, the cron scripts do not, so two variables could disagree; `researchDb` still refuses to open
when the two paths are equal, though this function never produces that):

```js
const path = require('path');
const resolveDbPath = require('./dbPath');

function deriveResearchPath(sessionsPath) {
  const dir = path.dirname(sessionsPath);
  const base = path.basename(sessionsPath);
  if (base.includes('sessions')) return path.join(dir, base.replaceAll('sessions', 'research'));
  const ext = path.extname(base);
  return path.join(dir, ext ? `${base.slice(0, -ext.length)}.research${ext}` : `${base}.research`);
}

function resolveResearchDbPath() {
  const editorialPath = resolveDbPath();
  return { researchPath: deriveResearchPath(editorialPath), editorialPath };
}

module.exports = { deriveResearchPath, resolveResearchDbPath };
```

Expected: PASS (the second is a regression control: `dbPath` is unchanged).

```bash
git commit -m "feat(server): derive the research file's path from sessions.db's (G1.2, spec §5.1)"
```

### Task 2: the research schema, outside `server/migrations/`

Spec §5.1 (Schema), §5.9 (columns), §5.10 (item columns), §5.16 (`editor_keys`); decision 23; Review Focus 20.

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: a better-sqlite3 handle (never opens one).
- Produces: `RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`; `ensureResearchSchema(db) → { ok: true, version: number } |
  { ok: false, code: string, error: string }`, never throws; it runs each step whose version exceeds
  `PRAGMA user_version`, each in a transaction that also sets `user_version`.

  `PRAGMA user_version` is 1; `table_info` names in order:
  - `research_events`: `['id', 'key_seq', 'event_type', 'editor_key', 'client_action_id', 'client_action_time_ms',
    'server_time_ms', 'replayed', 'session_id', 'book', 'chapter', 'module_id', 'segment_id', 'occurrence',
    'served_version_id', 'base_is_sha256', 'editing_kind', 'editing_id', 'editing_content_sha256', 'origin',
    'content_json', 'before_json', 'detail_json', 'flag_snapshot_json', 'server_commit', 'commit_source', 'code_dirty']`
  - `research_event_items`: `['event_id', 'ordinal', 'item_type', 'outcome', 'source', 'op_id', 'occurrence', 'category',
    'run_id', 'group_action_id', 'ruling_id', 'rule_id', 'client_time_ms', 'claim', 'detail_json']`
  - `editor_keys`: `['interval_id', 'editor_key', 'stratum']`
- `it('the idempotency and ordering keys')` — `index_list(research_events)` holds unique `idx_research_events_action`
  (columns `['editor_key', 'client_action_id']`, and its `sqlite_master.sql` contains `WHERE client_action_id IS NOT
  NULL`) and unique `idx_research_events_key_seq` (`['editor_key', 'key_seq']`). Behaviour, on a minted key `k`: two
  events `(k, client_action_id 'a')` → the second throws `/UNIQUE/`; two with `client_action_id NULL` and distinct
  `key_seq` insert (control: NULLs do not collide); two with the same `key_seq` throw `/UNIQUE/`.
- `it('the foreign keys inside research.db')` — an event whose `editor_key` has no `editor_keys` row throws
  `/FOREIGN KEY/`; deleting an event deletes its items (`ON DELETE CASCADE`: the item rows for it read `[]` after);
  deleting a key row while an event names it throws `/FOREIGN KEY/` (PR4 deletes the key last).
- `it('re-running on the result changes nothing')` — `sqlite_master` `sql` by `name` equal before and after a second call,
  which returns `{ ok: true, version: 1 }`.
- `it('never throws: a conflicting table rolls the step back and leaves user_version at 0')` — pre-create
  `research_events (x TEXT)`; result has `ok: false` and a string `error`; `user_version` 0; `editor_keys` absent.
  Control: the same call on a fresh file is ok.
- `it('a file newer than this code is not ok')` — `PRAGMA user_version = 2` on a fresh file → `ok: false`, `error`
  matching `/newer/`, and nothing created (`sqlite_master` empty).
- `it('a closed handle is not ok, and does not throw')` — `db.close()` first → `ok: false`.
- `it('lives outside server/migrations (spec §5.1)')` — no file in `server/migrations/` contains `research_events` or
  `editor_keys` (`fs.readFileSync`, so a NUL byte cannot hide a match); control: `server/lib/researchSchema.js` does.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it**

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchSchema.test.js`
Expected: FAIL — `Cannot find module '../lib/researchSchema'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `server/lib/researchSchema.js` (header: spec §5.1; why not in `server/migrations/`; why each
  step is gated by `user_version` inside one never-throw boundary; why a newer version is refused; that the foreign
  keys are live in this build, CLAUDE.md § Tech Stack).

```js
const RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1;
const HEX64 = (c) => `${c} IS NULL OR (length(${c}) = 64 AND ${c} NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*')`;

const STEPS = Object.freeze([
  {
    version: 1,
    apply(db) {
      db.exec(`
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS editor_keys (
          interval_id INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE,
          editor_key TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE
            CHECK (length(editor_key) = 32 AND editor_key NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
          stratum TEXT
        );
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS research_events (
          id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id > 0),          -- random 63-bit, never AUTOINCREMENT (spec §5.9)
          key_seq INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (key_seq >= 1),
          event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
          editor_key TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES editor_keys (editor_key),
          client_action_id TEXT,
          client_action_time_ms INTEGER,
          server_time_ms INTEGER NOT NULL,
          replayed INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (replayed IN (0, 1)),
          session_id TEXT,
          book TEXT NOT NULL,
          chapter INTEGER NOT NULL,
          module_id TEXT NOT NULL,
          segment_id TEXT,
          occurrence INTEGER,
          served_version_id INTEGER,                      -- a sessions.db id, plain integer: no cross-file key
          base_is_sha256 TEXT CHECK (${HEX64('base_is_sha256')}),
          editing_kind TEXT,
          editing_id INTEGER,
          editing_content_sha256 TEXT CHECK (${HEX64('editing_content_sha256')}),
          origin TEXT,
          content_json TEXT,
          before_json TEXT,
          detail_json TEXT,
          flag_snapshot_json TEXT NOT NULL,
          server_commit TEXT,
          commit_source TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (commit_source IN ('deploy-file', 'git-head', 'unknown')),
          code_dirty INTEGER CHECK (code_dirty IS NULL OR code_dirty IN (0, 1))
        );
        CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_research_events_action
          ON research_events (editor_key, client_action_id) WHERE client_action_id IS NOT NULL;
        CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_research_events_key_seq ON research_events (editor_key, key_seq);
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS research_event_items (
          event_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES research_events (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
          ordinal INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (ordinal >= 0),
          item_type TEXT NOT NULL,
          outcome TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (outcome IN ('applied', 'rejected', 'left-open')),
          source TEXT,
          op_id TEXT, occurrence INTEGER, category TEXT, run_id TEXT, group_action_id TEXT,
          ruling_id TEXT, rule_id TEXT,
          client_time_ms INTEGER,
          claim TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (claim IN ('validated', 'unverified', 'mismatch')),
          detail_json TEXT,
          PRIMARY KEY (event_id, ordinal)
        );
      `);
    },
  },
]);

function ensureResearchSchema(db) {
  try {
    // Number(): on researchDb's handle (defaultSafeIntegers) a pragma reads as a BigInt (measured: 0n).
    const current = Number(db.pragma('user_version', { simple: true }));
    if (current > RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION) {
      return { ok: false, code: 'SCHEMA_NEWER', error: `user_version ${current} is newer than ${RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION}` };
    }
    for (const step of STEPS) {
      if (step.version <= current) continue;
      db.transaction(() => {
        step.apply(db);
        db.pragma(`user_version = ${step.version}`);
      })();
    }
    return { ok: true, version: Number(db.pragma('user_version', { simple: true })) };
  } catch (err) {
    return { ok: false, code: err.code || err.name, error: err.message };
  }
}

module.exports = { RESEARCH_SCHEMA_VERSION, ensureResearchSchema };
```

(`PRAGMA user_version` is a header write inside the transaction, so a rolled-back step leaves it unchanged; the
"conflicting table" case proves it. The `error` text stays on the server: `researchDb` logs only `code`.)

case's filter reads `[]`, not `['research_consent', 'book_switch_events']` (the `DROP … IF EXISTS` drops nothing, and
the tables are not in `EXPECTED_SCHEMA`).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`server/migrations/055-research-consent-and-switches.js`. Header: what each table is (§5.11, §5.13, §5.14); why
`research_consent.user_id` has no foreign key (users can be hard-deleted with cascade; the record must outlive what it
proves, §5.14); why these live in `sessions.db` (the gate must work while the research file is down; the screen reads
the switch); why the research tables are NOT here (`freshMigratedDb` applies every file in this directory to
`sessions.db`, §5.1); why it never throws (CLAUDE.md § Tech Stack, `048`); that `checks.schema` reports a failure.
**The header names no `research.db` table** (say "the research file's tables"): Task 2's test reads every file in
this directory as bytes and fails on `research_events` or `editor_keys`.

```js
function migrate(db) {
  db.transaction(() => {
    db.exec(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS research_consent (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        user_id INTEGER NOT NULL,                       -- users.id, deliberately no FOREIGN KEY (spec §5.14)
        decision TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (decision IN ('consented', 'declined')),
        interval_id INTEGER CHECK (decision = 'consented' OR interval_id IS NULL),
        consent_text_version TEXT NOT NULL,
        consent_text_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL
          CHECK (length(consent_text_sha256) = 64 AND consent_text_sha256 NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
        given_at_ms INTEGER NOT NULL,
        withdrawn_at_ms INTEGER,
        deletion_done_at_ms INTEGER
      );
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_research_consent_user ON research_consent (user_id, id);
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS research_capture_losses (
        interval_id INTEGER,
        book TEXT NOT NULL,
        chapter INTEGER NOT NULL,
        module_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
        loss_day TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (loss_day GLOB '[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]-[0-9][0-9]'),
        error_class TEXT NOT NULL
          CHECK (error_class IN ('unavailable', 'busy', 'constraint', 'consent-unknown', 'other')),
        client_action_id TEXT,
        CHECK ((error_class = 'consent-unknown') = (interval_id IS NULL))
      );
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_research_capture_losses_interval
        ON research_capture_losses (interval_id, client_action_id);
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS book_switch_events (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        book TEXT NOT NULL,
        switch_name TEXT NOT NULL,
        value INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (value IN (0, 1)),
        switched_at_ms INTEGER NOT NULL
      );
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_book_switch_events_lookup ON book_switch_events (book, switch_name, id);
    `);
  })();
}
```

`up(db)`: the `053` boundary, message prefix `[055]`, saying what fails while it is incomplete (consent decisions
cannot be recorded and `record` writes `consent-unknown` losses; `checks.schema` and `checks.research_db` report it),
that it retries on every start, and that it is not thrown on purpose. Append the `require` to `migrationRunner.js`.
In `server/lib/schemaHealth.js`, add to `EXPECTED_SCHEMA.tables` the three tables with every column above, in that
order (deep-frozen like the rest), and a header line: "PR3 (spec §5.20): tables only, no index (decision 38)".

- [ ] **Step 4: Run them**

Expected: PASS (`startup.test.js:80-89` sees `055` follow `054` densely; `researchSchema.test.js`'s "lives outside
server/migrations" case still finds no research table named in this directory). Then `npm test`; diff the failing set by name against `main`'s: no new names.

### Task 5: the consent record and the loss ledger, `sessions.db` side

Spec §5.11 (the ledger), §5.14 (decisions, intervals, withdrawal); decisions 15, 17, 18, 27, 28, 29; Review Focus 5, 16.

**Files:**
- Create: `server/services/researchConsent.js` — `getSessionsDb()`, `decisionStateFor`, `recordDecision`, `withdraw`,
  `captureStateFor`, `anyConsentedRow`, `recordLoss`, `_setTestDb`
- Test: `server/__tests__/researchConsent.test.js` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `isApprovedText` (Task 4); `resolveDbPath()`; `sha256Text` (PR1 Task 1), in the test only.
- Produces (the handle is lazy, WAL, opened at the first call from `resolveDbPath()` read then, as
  `server/services/activityLog.js:55-73` does but without capturing the path at load):
  - `decisionStateFor(userId: number) → { state: 'undecided' | 'consented' | 'declined' | 'withdrawn', decidedTextVersion: string | null }`
  - `recordDecision({ userId, decision: 'consented' | 'declined', text: { version, sha256 }, nowMs }) →
    { outcome: 'started' | 'renewed' | 'unchanged' | 'withdrew' | 'declined', intervalId: number | null }` — one
    transaction; throws `TypeError` on an unapproved `text` or a bad `decision`, writing nothing. On `withdrew`,
    `intervalId` is the withdrawn interval's id (its starting row's `id`), never `null`: PR4's immediate deletion after
    a declined consent needs it.
  - `withdraw({ userId, nowMs }) → { withdrawn: boolean, intervalId: number | null }` — sets `withdrawn_at_ms` on the
    live interval's starting row only (`… AND withdrawn_at_ms IS NULL`, set once); inserts nothing.
  - `captureStateFor(userId) → { capturing: false } | { capturing: true, intervalId: number, startedAtMs: number }` —
    the latest row (highest `id`) is `consented`, its starting row (same user) is not withdrawn, and the latest row's
    `(consent_text_version, consent_text_sha256)` is approved. **Throws** when the read fails (`record` turns that into
    `consent-unknown`).
  - `anyConsentedRow() → boolean` — any `consented` row at all, withdrawn or not (§5.1's missing-file rule). Throws on a
    failed read.
  - `recordLoss({ intervalId, book, chapter, moduleId, eventType, errorClass, clientActionId, nowMs }) → boolean` — never
    throws; `loss_day` is the UTC date of `nowMs` (Iceland keeps UTC); with a `clientActionId`, a row already holding the
    same `(interval_id, client_action_id)` makes it write nothing and return `true`.
  - `getSessionsDb()` — the same handle, for `record`'s reads of `served_text_versions`, `book_switch_events` and
    `segment_edits` (decision 65's live-edit read, `othersLiveTexts`, Task 11).

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchConsent.test.js`
Expected: FAIL — `Cannot find module '../services/researchConsent'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `server/services/researchConsent.js` (header: spec §5.11, §5.14; why it lives in
  `sessions.db` — it gates every event even when the research file is down; why "current" is the highest id; that no
  route displays a decision but the caller's own; that `recordLoss` is never-throw and carries no content). The live
  interval, shared by every function:

```js
function latestAndLive(conn, userId) {
  const latest = conn
    .prepare('SELECT * FROM research_consent WHERE user_id = ? ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1')
    .get(userId);
  if (!latest || latest.decision !== 'consented') return { latest, live: null };
  const start = conn
    .prepare('SELECT * FROM research_consent WHERE id = ? AND user_id = ?')
    .get(latest.interval_id, userId);
  return { latest, live: start && start.withdrawn_at_ms === null ? start : null };
}
```

`recordDecision` runs `latestAndLive` and its writes in one `conn.transaction(…)()`; a starting row is inserted with
`interval_id NULL` and then `UPDATE research_consent SET interval_id = id WHERE id = ?` in the same transaction (the
`055` CHECK constrains only declined rows, so the brief NULL is admitted, and the UPDATE fills it before the commit:
no reader ever sees a starting row without its interval). A decline during a live interval returns `{ outcome:
'withdrew', intervalId: live.id }`, the starting row it marked.
`recordLoss` wraps its whole body in one `try { … } catch (err) { log.error({ errorCode: err.code || err.name },
'research loss ledger write failed (spec §5.11)'); return false; }` and inserts with `INSERT INTO
research_capture_losses (…) SELECT ?, ?, … WHERE ? IS NULL OR NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM research_capture_losses WHERE
interval_id IS ? AND client_action_id = ?)`.

### Task 6: `researchDb` — the lazy handle, its short wait, its file mode, its unavailable state, and the boot that never waits on it

Spec §5.1 (Connection, File mode, Boot, Missing file, Tests and E2E); T10 (the same-path half); decisions 3, 4, 22, 24;
Review Focus 1, 2, 7, 18, 20.

**Files:**
- Create: `server/services/researchDb.js` — `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS`, `RETRY_INTERVAL_MS`, `getHandle()`, `openAtBoot()`,
  `status()`, `noteFailure(err)`, `_setTestDb`, `_setTestPaths`, `_reset`
- Modify: `server/index.js` — the research boot block right after `failLoudOnMigrationErrors(…)` (`:43-45`)
- Modify: `server/e2e/playwright.config.js` — `E2E_RESEARCH_DB` beside `E2E_DB` (`:5`), the `rm -f` at `:35`: the boot
  now creates `e2e-research.db`, so the same commit clears it (decision 3)
- Test: `server/__tests__/researchDb.test.js` (new); `server/__tests__/researchDbPath.test.js` (Task 1's; one `describe`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `resolveResearchDbPath()` (Task 1), `ensureResearchSchema` (Task 2), `researchConsent.anyConsentedRow()` (Task 5).
- Produces:
  - `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS = 100`, `RETRY_INTERVAL_MS = 60_000` (enforceable values).
  - `getHandle() → Database` (WAL, `defaultSafeIntegers(true)`, busy timeout `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS`) or throws
    `Error { code: 'RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'unavailable' | 'missing' | 'consent-unavailable' | 'corrupt' | 'same-path' }`.
  - `openAtBoot() → { ok: true } | { ok: false, reason }` — never throws; always attempts once.
  - `status() → { state: 'unopened' | 'open' | 'unavailable', reason, lastAttemptAtMs: number | null, sticky: boolean }`.
  - `noteFailure(err)` — `SQLITE_NOTADB` / `SQLITE_CORRUPT*`: close the handle, sticky `corrupt`; `SQLITE_IOERR*`: close,
    `unavailable`, and `lastAttemptAtMs = Date.now()` so the reopen waits its minute (§5.1: "at most once a minute");
    anything else (`busy`, `constraint`): keep the handle.
  - `_reset()` (test-only; also "a restart") — closes any open handle and sets the state back to `unopened`
    (`lastAttemptAtMs null`, not sticky); it leaves `testPaths` and `testDb` as they are.
  - `_setTestPaths(p: { researchPath, editorialPath } | null)` (test-only) — calls `_reset()`, then sets the paths
    (`null` restores the derived ones), so the next `getHandle()` attempts an open against them at once. Without the
    reset, `getHandle()` would keep returning the handle it already holds and the new paths would never be read.

  `runAllMigrations()` in `beforeAll`; `researchPath = resolveResearchDbPath().researchPath`; `afterEach`:
  `researchDb._reset()`, `vi.useRealTimers()`, remove `research.db` and its `-wal`/`-shm`, `DELETE FROM research_consent`.
  A consent row is planted with `INSERT INTO research_consent (user_id, decision, interval_id, consent_text_version,
  consent_text_sha256, given_at_ms) VALUES (1, 'consented', 1, 'v', <64 a>, 1)` then `UPDATE … SET interval_id = id`.

- `it('creates the file 0600 when no consent exists, in WAL, with the short wait and safe integers')` — `openAtBoot()`
  equals `{ ok: true }`; `fs.statSync(researchPath).mode & 0o777` is `0o600`; after one insert through `getHandle()`,
  each of `-wal` and `-shm` that exists is `0o600` too; `h.pragma('journal_mode', { simple: true })` is `'wal'`;
  `BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS` equals `100` (the pin) and `h.pragma('busy_timeout', { simple: true })` equals
  `BigInt(BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS)`; `h.pragma('user_version', { simple: true })` equals `1n`. (Measured 2026-11-03: on a
  handle with `defaultSafeIntegers(true)` even a pragma reads as a `BigInt`, `100n` and `0n`; that is what proves safe
  integers are on.)
- `it('ids above 2^53 survive the round trip; a plain read would not (decision 4)')` — insert an `editor_keys` row and a

  without the row the file is created.
- `it('an unreadable consent table leaves the file uncreated')` — `ALTER TABLE research_consent RENAME TO rc_hidden`;
  `openAtBoot()` is `{ ok: false, reason: 'consent-unavailable' }`; no file; rename back.
- `it('retries at most once a minute, and success clears the state')` — `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ['Date'] })`,
  `vi.setSystemTime(T)`; consent row, no file: `getHandle()` throws, `status().lastAttemptAtMs === T`. Copy a golden
  research file in place (made by `ensureResearchSchema` on a scratch path in its default rollback-journal mode, the
  scratch handle closed before the copy, so the file holds the whole schema); at `T + 30_000` `getHandle()` still throws
  and `lastAttemptAtMs` is still `T` (no attempt); at `T + 60_000` it returns a handle and `status().state` is `'open'`.
- `it('a corrupt file stays unavailable until a restart')` — write 4 KiB of text to `researchPath`; `openAtBoot()` →
  `{ ok: false, reason: 'corrupt' }`, `status().sticky` true; replace it with the golden file; at `T + 120_000`
  `getHandle()` still throws `corrupt`; after `_reset()` (a restart) it opens.
- `it('noteFailure: corrupt is sticky, busy keeps the handle')` — open; `noteFailure({ code: 'SQLITE_BUSY' })` →
  `status().state` `'open'`; `noteFailure({ code: 'SQLITE_CORRUPT' })` → `unavailable`, sticky, `reason 'corrupt'`.
- `it('refuses a research path equal to the editorial one (spec §5.1; T10)')` — `_setTestPaths({ researchPath:
  editorialPath, editorialPath })`; `openAtBoot()` → `{ ok: false, reason: 'same-path' }`; the editorial file's
  `sqlite_master` names (read through a second connection) are equal before and after (no research table was created
  in it). Control: `_setTestPaths(null)` and it opens the derived file.
- `it('a newer schema is unavailable')` — a research file with `PRAGMA user_version = 2` → `openAtBoot()` → `{ ok:
  false, reason: 'unavailable' }`.
- `it('_setTestPaths drops an open handle, so the next getHandle uses the new paths')` — `openAtBoot()` is `{ ok: true }`
  and `h = getHandle()` is open; `_setTestPaths({ researchPath: '/nonexistent/dir/r.db', editorialPath })` →
  `h.open === false`, and `getHandle()` throws `code 'RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE'`. Control: `_setTestPaths(null)` →
  `getHandle()` returns an open handle on the derived file (`.name === researchPath`).
- `it('a file that cannot be created is unavailable, never a raw error (decision 20)')` — no consent row;
  `_setTestPaths({ researchPath: path.join(<tmp>, 'no-such-dir', 'r.db'), editorialPath })`; `getHandle()` throws an
  error whose `code` is `'RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE'` and `reason` `'unavailable'` (not the `ENOENT` `fs.openSync` raises),
  and `status()` reads `{ state: 'unavailable', reason: 'unavailable', … }` (the failure went through `fail()`). Control:
  the first case, where creation succeeds.

- `it('index.js opens the research file at boot, after the editorial migrations and before listening (spec §5.1)')` —
  `src = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, '..', 'index.js'), 'utf8')` contains `require('./services/researchDb')`;
  `[src.indexOf('openAtBoot('), src.indexOf('failLoudOnMigrationErrors('), src.indexOf('app.listen(')]` reads as
  `[b, f, l]` with `f < b && b < l` (the pin shape of `startup.test.js:163-171`); and the slice from `openAtBoot(` back
  to the nearest `try {` holds no `return` and no `process.exit` (the boot cannot be stopped by it).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `server/services/researchDb.js` (header: spec §5.1; the 5000 ms default is synchronous and
  blocks the event loop, so a short wait turns a blocked write into a counted loss; why the file is created only by
  `openSync(…, 0o600)` and only while no consent exists — a fresh file would re-key every editor and detach earlier
  data; why safe integers are on — decision 4; that routes never require this module, T17):

```js
const BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS = 100;
const RETRY_INTERVAL_MS = 60_000;
const STICKY = new Set(['corrupt', 'same-path']);
const unavailable = (reason) =>
  Object.assign(new Error(`research file unavailable: ${reason}`), { code: 'RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE', reason });
const isCorrupt = (code) => code === 'SQLITE_NOTADB' || /^SQLITE_CORRUPT/.test(code || '');

let state = { handle: null, reason: null, lastAttemptAtMs: null, sticky: false };
let testDb = null;
let testPaths = null;

function attemptOpen() {
  state.lastAttemptAtMs = Date.now();
  let db = null;
  // ONE try around everything after the timestamp: a creation failure (EACCES, ENOENT, ENOSPC) must leave through
  // fail() as RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE (decision 20), never escape getHandle() as a raw error with a stale reason.
  try {
    const { researchPath, editorialPath } = testPaths || resolveResearchDbPath();
    if (researchPath === editorialPath) return fail('same-path');
    if (!fs.existsSync(researchPath)) {
      let consented;
      try {
        consented = researchConsent.anyConsentedRow();
      } catch {
        return fail('consent-unavailable'); // cannot tell whether creating would re-key editors: do not create
      }
      if (consented) return fail('missing');
      fs.closeSync(fs.openSync(researchPath, 'a', 0o600));
    }
    for (const p of [researchPath, `${researchPath}-wal`, `${researchPath}-shm`]) {
      if (fs.existsSync(p)) fs.chmodSync(p, 0o600);
    }
    db = new Database(researchPath, { timeout: BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS, fileMustExist: true });
    db.defaultSafeIntegers(true);
    db.pragma('journal_mode = WAL');
    const schema = ensureResearchSchema(db);
    if (!schema.ok) {
      db.close();
      return fail(isCorrupt(schema.code) ? 'corrupt' : 'unavailable', schema.code);
    }
    state = { handle: db, reason: null, lastAttemptAtMs: state.lastAttemptAtMs, sticky: false };
    return { ok: true };
  } catch (err) {
    if (db && db.open) db.close();
    return fail(isCorrupt(err.code) ? 'corrupt' : 'unavailable', err.code || err.name);
  }
}

function fail(reason, errorCode) {
  state.handle = null;
  state.reason = reason;
  state.sticky = STICKY.has(reason);
  log.warn({ reason, errorCode: errorCode || null }, 'research file unavailable (spec §5.1)');
  return { ok: false, reason };
}

function getHandle() {
  if (testDb) return testDb;
  if (state.handle) return state.handle;
  if (state.sticky) throw unavailable(state.reason);
  if (state.lastAttemptAtMs !== null && Date.now() - state.lastAttemptAtMs < RETRY_INTERVAL_MS) {
    throw unavailable(state.reason || 'unavailable');
  }
  const r = attemptOpen();
  if (!r.ok) throw unavailable(r.reason);
  return state.handle;
}
```

`openAtBoot()` returns `attemptOpen()` inside a `try/catch` that maps any throw to `{ ok: false, reason: 'unavailable' }`.
`status()` reports `state` as `'open'` with a handle, `'unopened'` before any attempt, else `'unavailable'`. The seams:

```js
function _reset() {
  if (state.handle && state.handle.open) state.handle.close();
  state = { handle: null, reason: null, lastAttemptAtMs: null, sticky: false };
}
function _setTestPaths(p) {
  _reset(); // else getHandle() keeps returning the handle it holds and never reads the new paths
  testPaths = p;
}
```

`server/index.js`, right after the `failLoudOnMigrationErrors(…)` call (`:43-45`):

```js
// The research file (G1.2, spec §5.1): its schema runs after the editorial migrations. A failure marks research
// unavailable (record() then counts losses) and is logged; it NEVER stops this server starting.
try {
  const research = require('./services/researchDb').openAtBoot();
  if (!research.ok) log.warn({ reason: research.reason }, 'Research file unavailable at boot (spec §5.1)');
} catch (err) {
  log.error({ errorCode: err.code || err.name }, 'Research boot step failed; the server starts anyway (spec §5.1)');
}
```

`server/e2e/playwright.config.js`: after `E2E_DB`,
`const E2E_RESEARCH_DB = require('../lib/researchDbPath').deriveResearchPath(E2E_DB); // spec §5.1: the research file follows sessions.db`,
and the `rm -f` line becomes
`` `rm -f "${E2E_DB}" "${E2E_DB}-wal" "${E2E_DB}-shm" "${E2E_RESEARCH_DB}" "${E2E_RESEARCH_DB}-wal" "${E2E_RESEARCH_DB}-shm"; ` + ``.

```js
function memberOr403(req, res) {
  const user = userService.findByProviderId(req.user.id);
  if (!user) {
    res.status(403).json({ error: 'Visitor', message: CONSENT_MESSAGES.visitor, visitor: true });

};
```

að skrá ákvörðunina.' }` (decision 64, approved). `POST /consent/withdraw`: `memberOr403` → `researchConsent.withdraw(…)` → 200
`view`. Mount in `server/index.js`. Run `npm run docs:generate` and keep only the `docs/_generated/routes.md` change.

`server/services/researchLog.js` (header: the research service routes may call; write-only, §5.16; `record` follows in
Task 11):

```js
const crypto = require('crypto');
const log = require('../lib/logger');
const researchDb = require('./researchDb');

/** Mint the editor key for a consent interval (spec §5.16). Write-only: never returns the key. Never throws. */
function ensureKey(intervalId) {
  try {
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(intervalId) || intervalId < 1) return { status: 'failed' };
    const db = researchDb.getHandle();
    return db
      .transaction(() => {
        if (db.prepare('SELECT 1 FROM editor_keys WHERE interval_id = ?').get(intervalId)) return { status: 'exists' };
        db.prepare('INSERT INTO editor_keys (interval_id, editor_key) VALUES (?, ?)').run(
          intervalId,
          crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex')
        );
        return { status: 'minted' };
      })
      .immediate();
  } catch (err) {
    // record() mints the key at the interval's first event when this fails (spec §5.16).
    log.error({ errorCode: err.code || err.name }, 'Editor key not minted at consent (spec §5.16)');
    researchDb.noteFailure(err);
    return { status: 'failed' };
  }
}

module.exports = { ensureKey };
```

  `flagSnapshotJson(book, { conn })`, `scanEnvReads(root, files)`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `canonicalJson(v) → string` — `JSON.stringify` with object keys sorted at every depth; arrays keep their order.
  - `SWITCH_NAMES = ['suggestion-view']`; `currentSwitchValue → boolean` (the highest-`id` row's `value === 1`; no row
    is `false`); `firstSwitchOnMs → number | null` (the `switched_at_ms` of the lowest-`id` row with `value 1`).
  - `FLAGS: readonly { name, source, read(book, ctx) → boolean | string, bookSettingsColumn?, envNames? }[]`, names:
    `apply-hold`, `pipeline-hold`, `figure-review-closed`, `enforce-assignments`, `enforce-localization-review`,
    `auto-create-users`, `greynir`, `email`, `suggestion-view`, `study-capture`, `not-minted`.
  - `NOT_A_FLAG = { bookSettings: {}, env: { SMTP_PORT, SMTP_SECURE, SMTP_FROM, BASE_URL, ADMIN_EMAIL: <reason each> } }`.
  - `ENV_SCAN_FILES` (relative to `server/`): §5.13's nine, `routes/segment-editor.js`, `services/segmentEditorService.js`,
    `services/acceptanceService.js`, `services/propagationService.js`, `services/segmentParser.js`,
    `services/servedEdit.js`, `services/researchLog.js` (Task 7 created it), `lib/applyHold.js`, `lib/heldBookWrites.js`;
    plus `services/greynirEngine.js`, `services/notifications.js` (where the registered flags' reads live),
    `services/moduleOpenings.js` (PR2's handoff: it reads no `process.env`, and listing it guards a future read),
    `lib/featureFlags.js`, `lib/bookSwitches.js`, `lib/researchDbPath.js`, `services/researchDb.js`,
    `services/researchConsent.js` (decision 33). `lib/serverCommit.js` joins in Task 9 and `services/researchEmission.js`
    in Task 19, each in the commit that creates it (a listed file that does not exist fails the test).

```js
const lazy = (rel) => () => require(rel); // auth.js throws at load without JWT_SECRET (services/auth.js:28-30)
const FLAGS = Object.freeze([
  { name: 'apply-hold', source: 'lib/applyHold.faithfulWriteHold',

  { name: 'pipeline-hold', source: 'lib/heldBookWrites.pipelineHold',
    read: (book) => require('./heldBookWrites').pipelineHold(book) !== null },
  { name: 'figure-review-closed', source: 'lib/heldBookWrites.figureReviewClosed',
    read: (book) => require('./heldBookWrites').figureReviewClosed(book) !== null },
  { name: 'enforce-assignments', source: 'services/userService.isAssignmentEnforced',
    bookSettingsColumn: 'enforce_assignments',
    read: (book) => require('../services/userService').isAssignmentEnforced(book) },
  { name: 'enforce-localization-review', source: 'services/localizationReviewService.isReviewEnabled',
    bookSettingsColumn: 'enforce_localization_review',
    read: (book) => require('../services/localizationReviewService').isReviewEnabled(book) },
  { name: 'auto-create-users', source: 'services/auth.signInPolicy', envNames: ['AUTO_CREATE_USERS'],
    read: () => lazy('../services/auth')().signInPolicy(process.env.AUTO_CREATE_USERS) },
  { name: 'greynir', source: 'services/greynirEngine.isEnabled', envNames: ['GREYNIR_URL'],
    read: () => require('../services/greynirEngine').isEnabled() },
  { name: 'email', source: 'services/notifications.isEmailConfigured', envNames: ['SMTP_HOST', 'SMTP_USER', 'SMTP_PASS'],
    read: () => require('../services/notifications').isEmailConfigured() },
  { name: 'suggestion-view', source: 'lib/bookSwitches (book_switch_events)',
    read: (book, { conn }) => require('./bookSwitches').currentSwitchValue(conn, book, 'suggestion-view') },
  { name: 'study-capture', source: 'lib/researchScope.isStudyBook',
    read: (book) => require('./researchScope').isStudyBook(book) },
  { name: 'not-minted', source: 'lib/servedText.isNotMintedBook',
    read: (book) => require('./servedText').isNotMintedBook(book) },
]);
```

`NOT_A_FLAG.env` reasons: `SMTP_PORT`, `SMTP_SECURE`, `SMTP_FROM` — "transport settings of the email flag; whether
email is on is `email`"; `BASE_URL` — "the link base in notification bodies; it changes no behaviour on the editing
path"; `ADMIN_EMAIL` — "where feedback notifications go; not on the editing path". `scanEnvReads` reads each file with
`fs.readFileSync` (a NUL byte cannot hide a match), matches `/process\.env(?:\.([A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*)|\[\s*['"]([A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*)['"]\s*\])/g`,
and reports every other `process.env` occurrence (`/process\.env\b/g` minus those) as `<unparsed>`. Keep the word
`process.env` out of `featureFlags.js`'s comments (it scans itself).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`scripts/record-deployed-commit.sh`:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# record-deployed-commit.sh <upstream-ref> <out-file> — which code a deploy pulled (G1.2, spec §5.12).
# deploy.sh runs it right after `git pull --rebase origin main`, with FETCH_HEAD: the upstream commit is the code
# the server runs; HEAD also carries the content cron's commits rebased on top. server/lib/serverCommit.js reads
# the file once at boot. The old file is deleted FIRST, so a failure here leaves no stale commit behind and the
# server falls back to `git rev-parse HEAD` (commit_source git-head).
set -euo pipefail
ref="${1:?usage: record-deployed-commit.sh <upstream-ref> <out-file>}"
out="${2:?usage: record-deployed-commit.sh <upstream-ref> <out-file>}"
rm -f "$out"
upstream="$(git rev-parse --verify --quiet "${ref}^{commit}")"
head="$(git rev-parse --verify --quiet 'HEAD^{commit}')"
tmp="${out}.tmp.$$"
printf '{"upstream":"%s","head":"%s"}\n' "$upstream" "$head" > "$tmp"
mv -f "$tmp" "$out"
```

`scripts/deploy.sh`, after `git pull --rebase origin main`:

```bash

# 3a. Record which code this deploy pulled (G1.2, spec §5.12). Research events stamp it; without it the server
# falls back to HEAD, which also carries content commits. Never fails the deploy.
bash scripts/record-deployed-commit.sh FETCH_HEAD pipeline-output/.deployed-commit || echo "WARNING: could not record the deployed commit; research events will say git-head until the next deploy."
```

(One physical line, on purpose: the pin reads from the script name to the end of that line and requires the `||`
there. Prettier does not format `.sh` files, so nothing re-wraps it.)

`server/lib/serverCommit.js` (header: spec §5.12; why HEAD at write time is not the running code —
`scripts/deploy.sh:73` rebases content commits on top of it; the shape of `scripts/export-segment-edits.js:43-52`'s
never-throw git call, `resolveMainCommit`; what `code_dirty` cannot see, decision 36; that tests use
`makeServerCommitReader` on a temporary repository and never the production reader, spec T13):

```js
const SHA = /^[0-9a-f]{40}$/;
const REPO_ROOT = path.join(__dirname, '..', '..');
const DEPLOY_FILE = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'pipeline-output', '.deployed-commit');
const DIRTY_PATHS = ['server', 'tools/lib'];

function defaultRunGit(args, cwd) {
  const r = spawnSync('git', args, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 5000 });
  return { status: r.error ? null : r.status, stdout: r.stdout || '' };
}

function computeServerCommit({ repoRoot, deployFile, runGit = defaultRunGit }) {
  try {
    let serverCommit = null;
    let commitSource = 'unknown';
    try {
      const upstream = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(deployFile, 'utf8')).upstream;
      if (SHA.test(upstream)) [serverCommit, commitSource] = [upstream, 'deploy-file'];
    } catch {
      // no file, or not JSON: fall back to HEAD below (a restart without deploy.sh)
    }
    if (!serverCommit) {
      const head = runGit(['rev-parse', 'HEAD'], repoRoot);
      const sha = head.status === 0 ? head.stdout.trim() : '';
      if (SHA.test(sha)) [serverCommit, commitSource] = [sha, 'git-head'];
    }
    if (!serverCommit) return { serverCommit: null, commitSource: 'unknown', codeDirty: null };
    const diff = runGit(['diff', '--quiet', serverCommit, '--', ...DIRTY_PATHS], repoRoot);
    const codeDirty = diff.status === 0 ? 0 : diff.status === 1 ? 1 : null;
    return { serverCommit, commitSource, codeDirty };
  } catch {
    return { serverCommit: null, commitSource: 'unknown', codeDirty: null }; // never throws (spec §5.12)
  }
}

/** One memoized reader per root. The production one below is rooted at the real tree: no test calls it (T13). */
function makeServerCommitReader({ repoRoot, deployFile, runGit = defaultRunGit }) {
  let memo = null;
  return { get: () => memo || (memo = computeServerCommit({ repoRoot, deployFile, runGit })) };
}
const production = makeServerCommitReader({ repoRoot: REPO_ROOT, deployFile: DEPLOY_FILE });
let testValue = null;
const getServerCommit = () => testValue || production.get();
/** Test-only: a fixed value, so a test that reaches record() never runs git on the real tree. */
function _setTestServerCommit(v) {
  testValue = v;
}

module.exports = { REPO_ROOT, DEPLOY_FILE, computeServerCommit, makeServerCommitReader, getServerCommit, _setTestServerCommit };
```

`server/index.js`, right after Task 6's research boot block:

```js
// The running code's commit, read once (spec §5.12); research events stamp it.
try {
  const { serverCommit, commitSource, codeDirty } = require('./lib/serverCommit').getServerCommit();
  log.info({ serverCommit, commitSource, codeDirty }, 'Server commit (spec §5.12)');
} catch (err) {
  log.error({ errorCode: err.code || err.name }, 'Server commit could not be read (spec §5.12)');
}
```

`server/lib/featureFlags.js`: append `'lib/serverCommit.js'` to `ENV_SCAN_FILES` (it reads no `process.env`; listing it
keeps §5.1's "no second environment variable" checked).

  opts)`, `buildItemRows(eventType, items)`

  null`; `filterContent(c, { withheld }?) → string | null` (canonical JSON); `filterBefore(b, actor, { withheld }?) →
  string | null` (canonical JSON of an array of `{ table, row }`); `filterDetail(eventType, d, { withheld }?) → string
  | null` (canonical JSON); `buildItemRows(eventType, items) → row[]` (columns of `research_event_items` but
  `event_id`). `withheld` is a `Set` of texts other editors hold as live edits on the segments the event names

  rules (the build turns it into an `other` loss).

    'terminology', note: { length: 9, sha256: sha256Text('Sjá Jón 🙂') }, text: 'Ný þýðing' }` (length in code points:
    the emoji counts once); the stored string contains no `Jón` (control: it does contain `Ný þýðing`).
  - `comment` likewise; `note: ''` and `note: null` give `note: null`; `filterContent(null)` is `null`.
  - `filterContent({ text: 'x', editorNote: 'y' })` throws `TypeError` (an unknown key is never stored).
  - `it('decision 65: a text another editor holds as a live edit is stored as textSha256')` — `JSON.parse(filterContent(
    { text: 'Ný þýðing', category: 'terminology' }, { withheld: new Set(['Ný þýðing']) }))` equals `{ category:
    'terminology', textSha256: sha256Text('Ný þýðing') }`, and the stored string contains no `Ný þýðing`. Control: with
    `withheld: new Set(['Ný þýðing '])` (one trailing space: only byte-identical counts) the `text` is kept, as it is
    with no option. `filterContent({ textSha256: 'a'.repeat(64) })` throws `TypeError` (only `record` withholds).

    reviewer_note: { length: 4, sha256 }, created_at: '2026-11-01 10:00:00', applied: true } }]` — no `editor_id`,

  - `it('ids compare as strings, as acceptanceService does')` — `editor_id 42` (a number) with actor `'42'` keeps the text.
  - `it('decision 65: the author\'s own text that another editor holds live is stored as its hash')` — actor
    `'p-author'`, `{ withheld: new Set(['Texti höfundar']) }`: the row holds `edited_content_sha256:
    sha256Text('Texti höfundar')` and no `edited_content`; `created_at` is still kept (the time is the actor's).
    Control: the first case above, with no option, keeps `edited_content`.
  - `it('an acceptance row')` — `segment_acceptances` row with `accepted_by 'p-acc'`, `accepted_content 'MT'`,
    `accepted_at`, `superseded_at`, `superseded_reason 'edit'`, `applied_at null`: actor `'p-acc'` → `{ id, book,

  - `it('an array stays an array, a single entry becomes one; an unknown column is dropped; an unknown table throws')`.
  - `it('every column of both tables is classified (drift guard)')` — on `freshMigratedDb()`, for each table,

- `describe('filterDetail (decision 54\'s previewText; decision 65)')`:

    source: 'tm', op_id: null, occurrence: null, category: null, run_id: null, group_action_id: null, ruling_id: null,
    rule_id: null, client_time_ms: 5, claim: 'validated', detail_json: '{"sha256":"eee…","text":"Heill texti"}' }, {

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it**

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchFilter.test.js`
Expected: FAIL — `Cannot find module '../lib/researchVocabulary'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the two modules. `researchFilter.js`'s header carries the field table (spec §5.9 plus decision
  10's rows), says the drift guard is what makes a new editing column a decision rather than an accident, that
  `filterBefore` is the only place a before-image is shaped (`record` calls it; no caller can forget), and that
  `withheld` is decision 65's set, computed by `record` from `sessions.db` (this module reads no database). The
  classes:

```js
const BEFORE_FIELD_CLASSES = Object.freeze({
  segment_edits: {
    id: 'locator', book: 'locator', chapter: 'locator', module_id: 'locator', segment_id: 'locator',
    original_content: 'base', edited_content: 'text', category: 'state', editor_note: 'free-text',
    status: 'state', editor_id: 'identity', editor_username: 'identity', reviewer_id: 'identity',
    reviewer_username: 'identity', reviewer_note: 'free-text', created_at: 'time:editor_id',
    reviewed_at: 'time:reviewer_id', applied_at: 'applied', review_id: 'dropped',
    served_version_id: 'state', occurrence: 'state', origin: 'state',
    displayed_edit_id: 'state', displayed_edit_sha256: 'state',
  },
  segment_acceptances: {
    id: 'locator', book: 'locator', chapter: 'locator', module_id: 'locator', segment_id: 'locator',
    accepted_content: 'base', accepted_by: 'identity', accepted_by_username: 'identity',
    accepted_at: 'time:accepted_by', status: 'state', superseded_at: 'dropped', superseded_reason: 'state',
    applied_at: 'applied', served_version_id: 'state', occurrence: 'state',
  },
});

const NONE_WITHHELD = Object.freeze(new Set());

function filterRow(table, row, actor, withheld = NONE_WITHHELD) {
  const classes = BEFORE_FIELD_CLASSES[table];
  if (!classes) throw new TypeError(`filterBefore: unknown table ${table}`);
  const own = (col) => row[col] !== null && row[col] !== undefined && String(row[col]) === actor;
  const out = {};
  for (const [col, cls] of Object.entries(classes)) {
    if (!(col in row)) continue;
    if (cls === 'locator' || cls === 'state') out[col] = row[col];
    else if (cls === 'free-text') out[col] = freeText(row[col]);
    else if (cls === 'applied') out.applied = row[col] !== null;
    else if (cls === 'text') {
      // Kept only when the actor wrote it AND no other editor holds the same bytes as a live edit (decision 65).
      if (own('editor_id') && !withheld.has(row[col])) out.edited_content = row[col];
      else out.edited_content_sha256 = row[col] === null ? null : sha256Text(row[col]);
    } else if (cls.startsWith('time:') && own(cls.slice(5))) out[col] = row[col];
    // 'base', 'identity', 'dropped', and another person's time: left out (spec §5.9; decisions 9–10)
  }
  return out;
}
```

`filterContent(content, { withheld = NONE_WITHHELD } = {})` keeps decision 11's allowlist (so a caller's `textSha256`
throws like any unknown key) and, when `typeof content.text === 'string' && withheld.has(content.text)`, writes
`textSha256: sha256Text(content.text)` in place of `text`. `filterBefore(before, actorProviderId, { withheld =
NONE_WITHHELD } = {})` returns `null` for a `null` before-image, else `canonicalJson` of the array (a single entry
becomes one) with each `{ table, row }` mapped to `{ table, row: filterRow(table, row, actorProviderId, withheld) }`.
`DETAIL_TEXT_KEYS = Object.freeze({ propagation: Object.freeze(['previewText']) })`; `filterDetail(eventType, detail,
{ withheld = NONE_WITHHELD } = {})` returns `null` for a `null` detail, else `canonicalJson` of a shallow copy without
each key `DETAIL_TEXT_KEYS[eventType]` lists whose value `withheld` has (the hash beside it, `previewSha256`, stays).
All three default `withheld` to `NONE_WITHHELD`, so a call with no option keeps every text (Step 1's no-option
controls; a bare `{ withheld } = {}` would leave it `undefined` and throw on `.has`).

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchFilter.test.js`
Expected: PASS.

```bash
git commit -m "feat(server): the research vocabularies and the filter rule — content, before-images, details, items (§5.9, §5.10)"
```

`server/__tests__/researchAppendOnly.test.js`:

```js
const TABLES = ['research_events', 'research_event_items', 'editor_keys'];
const MUTATION = new RegExp(
  String.raw`\b(?:UPDATE\s+(?:OR\s+\w+\s+)?|REPLACE\s+INTO\s+|INSERT\s+OR\s+REPLACE\s+INTO\s+|DELETE\s+FROM\s+)(${TABLES.join('|')})\b`,
  'gi'
);
// An upsert's UPDATE is followed by SET, not a table name, so MUTATION cannot see it (the form
// userService.js:589-595 uses). STMT is a tempered character: it never crosses into another INSERT or another
// prepare(/exec( call, so a match stays inside one statement. Reported as `UPSERT <table>`.
const STMT = String.raw`(?:(?!\bINSERT\b|\bprepare\(|\bexec\()[\s\S])`;
const UPSERT = new RegExp(
  String.raw`\bINSERT\s+(?:OR\s+\w+\s+)?INTO\s+(${TABLES.join('|')})\b${STMT}{0,800}?\bON\s+CONFLICT\b${STMT}{0,200}?\bDO\s+UPDATE\b`,
  'gi'
);
// Files allowed to DELETE research rows (spec §5.15), and ONLY from the tables listed: an UPDATE, an upsert, or a
// DELETE from an unlisted table in an allowed file is still a finding. Keys are paths relative to server/; each entry
// is { tables: string[], reason: string }. Empty in PR3; PR4 adds research-withdrawal.js, research-retention.js and the
// withdraw route's deletion service, each with its tables and its reason, and changes nothing else here.
const DELETE_ALLOWED = Object.freeze({});
const isAllowed = (f, allowed) =>
  Boolean(allowed[f.file]) && allowed[f.file].tables.some((t) => f.statement === `DELETE FROM ${t}`);
function mutations(root, allowed = DELETE_ALLOWED) { /* walk root (skip node_modules, .venv, __tests__),
                              fs.readFileSync each .js/.cjs/.mjs, collect [{ file, statement }] for every MUTATION
                              match (statement `<VERB> <table>`, e.g. `DELETE FROM editor_keys`) and every UPSERT
                              match (statement `UPSERT <table>`), and return those for which !isAllowed(f, allowed) */ }
```

- `it('no code changes a research row, and nothing deletes one yet')` — `mutations(SERVER_ROOT)` equals `[]`.
- `it('control: a planted UPDATE, DELETE and upsert are named; a DO NOTHING insert is not')` — a temp root with
  `services/x.js` holding `db.prepare('UPDATE research_events SET event_type = ?')`, `"DELETE FROM editor_keys"`,
  `` db.prepare(`INSERT INTO editor_keys (interval_id, editor_key) VALUES (?, ?)\n  ON CONFLICT (interval_id) DO
  UPDATE SET editor_key = excluded.editor_key`) `` and `db.prepare("INSERT INTO research_events (id) VALUES (?) ON
  CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING")` → the findings' `statement`s, sorted, equal `['DELETE FROM editor_keys', 'UPDATE
  research_events', 'UPSERT editor_keys']`, all with `file 'services/x.js'` (the `DO NOTHING` insert changes no row,
  so it is not one; measured on a scratch
  copy of the two patterns below: the tempered span keeps a match inside one statement, where an untempered
  `[\s\S]*?` reached the next statement's `DO UPDATE`).
- `it('control: an allowed file deletes only from its listed tables; its UPDATE is still a finding')` — a temp root
  with `services/x.js` holding `"DELETE FROM research_events"`, `"DELETE FROM editor_keys"` and `db.prepare('UPDATE
  research_events SET event_type = ?')`: `mutations(tmp, { 'services/x.js': { tables: ['research_events'], reason:
  'control' } })`'s `statement`s, sorted, equal `['DELETE FROM editor_keys', 'UPDATE research_events']`; and, in the
  same fixture, `mutations(tmp)` (the real list, empty) equals those plus `'DELETE FROM research_events'` (the
  positive control: the allowance is what removed it).
- `it('the scanner reads the real writer (aimed right)')` — `services/researchLog.js`'s source matches
  `/INSERT INTO research_events/` (so a scan that never opened it cannot read as clean).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `server/services/researchLog.js` (header: spec §5.11's order — the editing record first,
  then this call, synchronously, inside the request, never affecting the response; G1.23 calls it and never checks
  consent itself; the gate drops decliners and undecided editors before anything is written or counted, R1; services
  expose only write functions to routes, §5.16; call it bare, it cannot throw).

```js
function record(event) {
  // A plain copy of the top-level fields, taken FIRST and inside its own try: a getter or a proxy that throws is met
  // here, once. Everything after reads only this copy, so the catch below (and Task 12's lose / consentUnknown, which
  // read ev.book, ev.chapter, …) cannot throw by reading the field that failed. Nested values (content, before, …) are
  // read inside the build, whose failures Task 12 catches.
  let ev;
  try {
    ev = event !== null && typeof event === 'object' ? { ...event } : {};
  } catch (err) {
    logLoss(err, null, 'other');
    return { status: 'lost', reason: 'other', ledgered: false };
  }
  try {
    return recordInner(ev);
  } catch (err) {
    logLoss(err, ev, 'other');
    return { status: 'lost', reason: 'other', ledgered: false };
  }
}

function recordInner(ev) {
  if (!isStudyBook(ev.book)) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'not-study' };          // step 1
  // Task 12 wraps steps 2–3 (consent-unknown) and steps 4–5 (a counted loss); until then a throw reaches record()'s
  // catch. No catch-and-rethrow here: eslint's no-useless-catch (js.configs.recommended) refuses it.
  const user = userService.findByProviderId(ev.actorProviderId);                         // step 2
  if (!user) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'no-user' };
  const capture = researchConsent.captureStateFor(user.id);                              // step 3
  consentFlag = false;
  if (!capture.capturing) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'not-consenting' };
  if (ev.replayed === true && !(Number.isFinite(ev.clientActionTime) && ev.clientActionTime >= capture.startedAtMs)) {
    return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'before-consent' };
  }
  const row = buildEvent(ev);                                                            // step 4
  const items = buildItemRows(ev.eventType, ev.items);
  return writeEvent(capture.intervalId, row, items);                                     // step 5
}
```

`buildEvent` validates (`eventType` in `EVENT_TYPES`; integer `chapter`; string `moduleId`; `segmentId` string or null;
`occurrence`, `servedVersionId`, `editingId` integers or null; `editingKind` in `EDITING_KINDS` or null, set together
with `editingId`; `editingContentSha256` 64-hex or null; `origin` in `EDIT_ORIGINS` ∪ `RESERVED_EDIT_ORIGINS` or null)
and assembles, before any research lock is taken: `base_is_sha256` from `SELECT is_sha256 FROM served_text_versions
WHERE id = ?` on `getSessionsDb()` (a missing row throws); `withheld = othersLiveTexts(ev)` (decision 65, below); the
content, before-image and detail through Task 10's `filterContent(ev.content, { withheld })`, `filterBefore(ev.before,
ev.actorProviderId, { withheld })` and `filterDetail(ev.eventType, ev.detail, { withheld })`; the flag snapshot; the
server commit; `client_action_id` (a 1–128 character string, else null), `client_action_time_ms` (`Math.trunc` of a
finite number, else null), `replayed` (`ev.replayed === true ? 1 : 0`), `session_id` (string or null), `server_time_ms`
(`Date.now()`).

```js
// Decision 65 (spec AMENDED 2026-11-04): the texts OTHER editors hold as live edits on the segments this event names:
// its own segment, and a propagation's source (detail.source, decision 54). "Live" is
// propagationService.latestEditedText's predicate, verbatim (the test pins the string). Read on the sessions.db
// connection after the act committed: the route is synchronous from its write to emit, and no save or propagation
// changes another editor's row, so these are the rows the act met. Runs after the gate (step 4), so a decliner's,
// a visitor's or a non-study request never pays for it; a throw here is a build failure (a counted loss, Task 12).
const LIVE_EDITS_SQL = `SELECT editor_id, edited_content FROM segment_edits
   WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND status NOT IN ('rejected', 'superseded')`;
function othersLiveTexts(ev) {
  const named = [[ev.moduleId, ev.segmentId]];
  const src = ev.eventType === 'propagation' && ev.detail ? ev.detail.source : null;
  if (src) named.push([src.moduleId, src.segmentId]);
  const withheld = new Set();
  const stmt = researchConsent.getSessionsDb().prepare(LIVE_EDITS_SQL);
  for (const [moduleId, segmentId] of named) {
    if (typeof moduleId !== 'string' || typeof segmentId !== 'string') continue; // an event that names no segment
    for (const r of stmt.all(ev.book, moduleId, segmentId)) {
      // decision 8's comparison; a row with no editor_id is another person's (String(null) is never an actor)
      if (String(r.editor_id) !== String(ev.actorProviderId) && typeof r.edited_content === 'string') {
        withheld.add(r.edited_content);
      }
    }
  }
  return withheld;
}
```

```js
function writeEvent(intervalId, row, items) {
  const db = researchDb.getHandle();
  const s = statementsFor(db); // prepared once per handle (a WeakMap)
  return db.transaction(() => {
    let key = s.selectKey.get(intervalId)?.editor_key;
    if (!key) {
      key = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
      s.insertKey.run(intervalId, key); // minted at the first event if minting at consent failed (§5.16)
    }
    if (row.client_action_id !== null && s.selectAction.get(key, row.client_action_id)) {
      return { status: 'duplicate' };
    }
    const keySeq = Number(s.maxSeq.get(key).m) + 1; // SELECT COALESCE(MAX(key_seq), 0) AS m …: a BigInt here
    const id = randomEventId();
    s.insertEvent.run({ ...row, id, editor_key: key, key_seq: keySeq });
    items.forEach((it) => s.insertItem.run({ ...it, event_id: id }));
    return { status: 'written', eventId: id };
  }).immediate();
}
```

`logLoss` is the one failure log (decision 21), defined here so `record`'s outer catch has it:

```js
function logLoss(err, ev, errorClass) {
  // ONLY these four fields (spec §5.11 step 2): no content, before-image, detail, key or user id reaches journald.
  log.error(
    { errorCode: (err && (err.code || err.name)) || null, errorClass, eventType: ev?.eventType ?? null, book: ev?.book ?? null },
    'research capture lost (spec §5.11)'
  );
}
```

`let consentFlag = false;` at module level; `consentLookupFailing()` returns it; `_reset()` clears it. `ensureKey` stays
as Task 7 left it. `module.exports = { record, ensureKey, consentLookupFailing, _reset }`.

arms; consent-unknown), T10 (record level: down, busy), T18 (event half, the file down); decisions 14, 17–21; Review
Focus 1, 2, 3, 9, 21, 40, 42.

  ledgered: boolean }`; `consentLookupFailing() → boolean`. A head carrying `buildFailed: true` (the emission's `emit`,

- `describe('record never throws')` — `record()`, `record(null)`, `record({})`, and an event whose `book` getter throws:

Expected: FAIL — every `lost` case returns `{ status: 'lost', reason: 'other', ledgered: false }` from `record`'s outer
catch (Task 11 has no inner handler), and no loss row is written; `consentLookupFailing()` stays `false`. The cases
that assert no loss (the decliner, the non-study act, the missing `users` table) and the throwing-getter case

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** in `researchLog.js`:

```js
function classifyError(err) {
  const code = (err && err.code) || '';
  if (code === 'RESEARCH_UNAVAILABLE') return 'unavailable';
  if (/^SQLITE_(BUSY|LOCKED)/.test(code)) return 'busy';
  if (/^SQLITE_CONSTRAINT/.test(code)) return 'constraint';
  return 'other';
}

function lose(err, ev, intervalId) {
  const errorClass = classifyError(err);
  researchDb.noteFailure(err);
  logLoss(err, ev, errorClass);
  const ledgered = researchConsent.recordLoss({
    intervalId, book: ev.book, chapter: ev.chapter, moduleId: ev.moduleId, eventType: ev.eventType,
    errorClass, clientActionId: typeof ev.clientActionId === 'string' ? ev.clientActionId : null, nowMs: Date.now(),
  });
  return { status: 'lost', reason: errorClass, ledgered };
}

function consentUnknown(err, ev) {
  consentFlag = true; // /api/health reports it (spec §5.20) until a lookup succeeds again
  logLoss(err, ev, 'consent-unknown');
  const ledgered = researchConsent.recordLoss({
    intervalId: null, book: ev.book, chapter: ev.chapter, moduleId: ev.moduleId, eventType: ev.eventType,
    errorClass: 'consent-unknown', clientActionId: typeof ev.clientActionId === 'string' ? ev.clientActionId : null,
    nowMs: Date.now(),
  });
  return { status: 'lost', reason: 'consent-unknown', ledgered };
}
```

(`logLoss` is Task 11's. `lose` and `consentUnknown` read `ev.book`, `ev.chapter`, … from the plain copy `record`
takes at its entry, Task 11, so a field that throws when read cannot make them throw.) `recordInner` becomes, with its
two failure handlers and the `buildFailed` check:

```js
function recordInner(ev) {
  if (!isStudyBook(ev.book)) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'not-study' };          // step 1
  let user;
  let capture;
  try {
    user = userService.findByProviderId(ev.actorProviderId);                            // step 2
    if (!user) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'no-user' };
    capture = researchConsent.captureStateFor(user.id);                                 // step 3
  } catch (err) {
    return consentUnknown(err, ev); // decision 17
  }
  consentFlag = false;
  if (!capture.capturing) return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'not-consenting' };
  if (ev.replayed === true && !(Number.isFinite(ev.clientActionTime) && ev.clientActionTime >= capture.startedAtMs)) {
    return { status: 'skipped', reason: 'before-consent' };
  }
  if (ev.buildFailed === true) {
    // The route's builder threw before record could build anything (Task 20's emit; decision 14). After the gate, so
    // only a consenting actor's failure is counted (R1); a step-4 failure in all but name (spec §5.11 step 6).
    return lose(Object.assign(new Error('research event build failed'), { code: 'BUILD_FAILED' }), ev, capture.intervalId);
  }
  try {
    const row = buildEvent(ev);                                                         // step 4
    const items = buildItemRows(ev.eventType, ev.items);
    return writeEvent(capture.intervalId, row, items);                                  // step 5
  } catch (err) {
    return lose(err, ev, capture.intervalId);
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run it**

Run: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/researchLogFailures.test.js server/__tests__/researchLog.test.js`
Expected: PASS.

```bash

git commit -m "feat(server): a failed research write is one counted loss, only for consenting editors (spec §5.11)"
```

```js
  // The research file (G1.2, spec §5.20): { ok, reason } and nothing else — this endpoint is public, and a count of
  // anything research-related would tell the world that someone consented or withdrew.
  try {
    const { readResearchDbHealth } = require('./lib/researchDbHealth');
    const { resolveResearchDbPath } = require('./lib/researchDbPath');
    const { consentLookupFailing } = require('./services/researchLog');
    checks.research_db = readResearchDbHealth({
      ...resolveResearchDbPath(),
      consentLookupFailing: consentLookupFailing(),

    checks.research_db = { ok: false, reason: 'unavailable' }; // never an error message: the endpoint is public
  }
```

Expected: FAIL — `r.before` is `undefined` (re-save and withdraw); `r.supersededEditIds` is `undefined`;
`supersedeForEdit` returns `undefined`. The empty-withdraw shape passes already (unchanged).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`supersedeForEdit`:

```js
function supersedeForEdit(book, moduleId, segmentId, dbConn = getDb()) {
  // G1.2 (spec §5.9): the ids are read in the caller's transaction, before the UPDATE, for the save's event.
  return dbConn.transaction(() => {
    const ids = dbConn
      .prepare(
        `SELECT id FROM segment_acceptances
         WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND status = 'active' ORDER BY id`
      )
      .all(book, moduleId, segmentId)
      .map((r) => r.id);
    dbConn.prepare(/* the existing UPDATE, unchanged */).run(book, moduleId, segmentId);
    return ids;
  })();
}
```

`saveSegmentEdit`: one helper for the editor's own stale rows, so both paths read the same predicate they update:

```js
  // The caller's own discuss/rejected rows on this segment: read, then superseded, in the caller's transaction.
  const STALE_OWN = `book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status IN ('discuss', 'rejected')`;
  const supersedeOwnStale = () => {
    const ids = conn.prepare(`SELECT id FROM segment_edits WHERE ${STALE_OWN} ORDER BY id`)
      .all(book, moduleId, segmentId, editorId).map((r) => r.id);
    conn.prepare(`UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' WHERE ${STALE_OWN}`).run(book, moduleId, segmentId, editorId);
    return ids;
  };
```

- Withdraw: `const before = conn.transaction(() => { const row = conn.prepare('SELECT * FROM segment_edits WHERE id
  = ?').get(existing.id); conn.prepare('DELETE FROM segment_edits WHERE id = ?').run(existing.id); return row;
  })();` then `return { id: existing.id, updated: true, reverted: true, before };`.
- `updateWithSupersede` returns `{ before, supersededAcceptanceIds, supersededEditIds }`: `before` is `SELECT *` of
  `existing.id` as its first statement, then `acceptanceService.supersedeForEdit(…, conn)`, then
  `supersedeOwnStale()`, then PR1 Task 12's UPDATE, unchanged. The return becomes `{ id: existing.id, updated: true,
  ...captured }`.
- `insertWithSupersede` likewise returns `{ result, supersededAcceptanceIds, supersededEditIds }`; the function's last
  return becomes `{ id: result.lastInsertRowid, updated: false, supersededEditIds, supersededAcceptanceIds }`.

A comment above each read: *G1.2 (spec §5.9): read inside the write's transaction; the research event filters it
(`record`), so this returns the raw row.*

`saveOnServedBase` (PR1 Task 13): after `const r = segmentEditor.saveSegmentEdit({…})`, the returned object gains
`before: r.before ?? null`, `supersededEditIds: r.supersededEditIds ?? []`, `supersededAcceptanceIds:
r.supersededAcceptanceIds ?? []`.

`server/__tests__/saveSegmentEditServedFields.test.js`, `server/__tests__/servedEdit.test.js`,
`server/__tests__/segmentEditorService.test.js`, `server/__tests__/errorHandling.test.js`,

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Each of the five review functions keeps its body, moved inside `conn.transaction(() => { … }).immediate()`, whose
first statement is today's `const edit = SELECT *` (that row is the before-image) and whose last line is `return {
...conn.prepare('SELECT * FROM segment_edits WHERE id = ?').get(editId), before: edit };`. A comment: *G1.2 (spec
§5.9): the before-image is read in the write's own transaction; IMMEDIATE takes the write lock before the read, so
the read is never followed by a failed upgrade (decision 45).* `deleteSegmentEdit` the same, returning `{ deleted:
true, before: edit }`. `revokeAcceptance` the same, returning `{ ...after, before: row }`.

`submitModuleForReview`: the stamp predicate becomes one constant read by both statements, and the transaction reads
the ids it is about to stamp:

> **Amended 2026-11-04 (§C209 follow-up (a)):** the stamp is per chapter now. `chapter-metadata` is one module id
> shared by every chapter, so a module-wide stamp let a ch02 review claim ch05's edits. `STAMP_WHERE` therefore
> carries `chapter = ?` and both statements bind `(…, book, chapter, moduleId)`. The snippet below says so; copying
> the approved `book = ? AND module_id = ?` form would undo that fix, and
> `server/__tests__/chapterMetadataReviewVersions.test.js` would turn red.

```js
  const STAMP_WHERE = `book = ? AND chapter = ? AND module_id = ?
       AND status NOT IN ('rejected', 'superseded')
       AND (review_id IS NULL OR status IN ('pending', 'discuss'))`;
  const stampEdits = conn.prepare(`UPDATE segment_edits SET review_id = ? WHERE ${STAMP_WHERE}`);
  const toStamp = conn.prepare(`SELECT id FROM segment_edits WHERE ${STAMP_WHERE} ORDER BY id`);
  const { reviewId, stampedEditIds } = conn.transaction(() => {
    const result = insertReview.run(…);                                   // unchanged
    const ids = toStamp.all(book, chapter, moduleId).map((r) => r.id);    // G1.2: the module-submit event's detail
    stampEdits.run(result.lastInsertRowid, book, chapter, moduleId);
    return { reviewId: result.lastInsertRowid, stampedEditIds: ids };
  })();
  return { id: reviewId, editedSegments: editCounts.pending_edits, stampedEditIds };
```

`addDiscussionComment`: `return { id: result.lastInsertRowid, edit };` (the row it already reads, `:780`).

`server/routes/segment-editor.js`, beside `sendRefusal`:

```js
// G1.2 (spec §5.9, §11): services hand the research event its before-images under these keys; no response carries
// them. A copy, so the caller's object (the research event's input) keeps them.
function withoutResearchKeys(result) {
  const rest = { ...result };
  delete rest.before;
  delete rest.writes;
  return rest;
}
```

(`delete` on a copy rather than `const { before, ...rest }`: `eslint.config.js:40` sets no `ignoreRestSiblings`, so an
unused `before` binding fails `npm run lint`.) In approve, reject, discuss, unapprove and return-to-pending,
`const edit = segmentEditor.approveEdit(…);` (and the other four) becomes `const result = segmentEditor.approveEdit(…);
const edit = withoutResearchKeys(result);`, everything after unchanged (Task 23 reads `result.before`). In revoke,
`const row = acceptanceService.revokeAcceptance(…);` becomes `const revoked = acceptanceService.revokeAcceptance(…);
const row = withoutResearchKeys(revoked);`.

`segmentEditorService.js`:

```js
const SUPERSEDED_BY_NEWER_NOTE = 'Leyst úr gildi af nýrri samþykktri breytingu';
```

and `markSuperseded` binds it: `` `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded', reviewer_note = ?, applied_at =
CURRENT_TIMESTAMP WHERE id = ?` `` run with `(SUPERSEDED_BY_NEWER_NOTE, id)`. The self-heal:

```js
      // G1.2 (spec §5.9): the rows the reset clears are read in the reset's own transaction, and the two UPDATEs
      // now commit together (decision 47). The rows ride on the re-run's result for the apply event.
      const selfHealReset = conn
        .transaction(() => {
          const edits = conn.prepare(
            `SELECT * FROM segment_edits
             WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND status = 'approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL ORDER BY id`
          ).all(book, moduleId);
          const acceptances = conn.prepare(
            `SELECT * FROM segment_acceptances
             WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND status = 'active' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL ORDER BY id`
          ).all(book, moduleId);
          /* the two existing UPDATEs, unchanged */
          return { edits, acceptances };
        })
        .immediate();
      try {
        const result = applyApprovedEdits(book, chapter, moduleId, options);
        _applyRetryState.delete(retryKey);
        result.before.selfHealReset = selfHealReset;
        return result;
      } catch (err) { /* unchanged */ }
```

In `applyTransaction`: `allApproved` selects `*` (a superset of the five columns the code reads); after the winner
loop, `const byId = new Map(allApproved.map((e) => [e.id, e]));`; the lapse line becomes `const lapsedAcceptanceIds =
acceptanceService.lapseDriftedIds(book, moduleId, segments, conn); const lapsedAcceptances =
lapsedAcceptanceIds.length;`; just before `const acceptedCount = acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn);`
(`:1084`, unchanged), `const stampedAcceptanceIds = conn.prepare("SELECT id FROM segment_acceptances WHERE book = ? AND
module_id = ? AND status = 'active' AND applied_at IS NULL ORDER BY id").all(book, moduleId).map((r) => r.id);` (the
exact predicate of `stampApplied`'s `UPDATE`, `acceptanceService.js:328-335`, on the same connection in the same
transaction, so the list is the rows the count counts); the return adds:

```js
      before: {
        applied: newlyApplied,                                  // as read above, before markApplied
        superseded: supersededIds.map((id) => byId.get(id)),    // reviewer_note as it was, before markSuperseded
        selfHealReset: { edits: [], acceptances: [] },          // set by the self-heal's caller frame
        lapsedAcceptanceIds,
        stampedAcceptanceIds,                                   // read just before stampApplied (decision 48)
      },
```

`contentVersionService.js`: declare `let lapsedAcceptanceIds = [];` before the `if (cfg.runPostWriteHooks)` block;
inside the existing `try` at `:321-326`, the **assignment** `lapsedAcceptanceIds = acceptanceService.lapseDriftedIds(book,
moduleId, restoredSegments);` replaces the `lapseDrifted` call. An assignment, not a `const`: a `const` there would
shadow the outer `let`, so the result would always read `[]`, and `npm run lint` would refuse the never-reassigned
`let` (`prefer-const`, `eslint.config.js:42`). The result gains `...(cfg.runPostWriteHooks ? { before: {
lapsedAcceptanceIds } } : {})`. Export `SUPERSEDED_BY_NEWER_NOTE` and `lapseDriftedIds`.

`server/routes/segment-editor.js` (decision 44): apply's `const result = segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(…);` becomes
`const applied = segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(…); const result = withoutResearchKeys(applied);`; apply-and-render's
`const applyResult = …` becomes `const applyRaw = …; const applyResult = withoutResearchKeys(applyRaw);`;
review-complete's `applied = segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(…);` becomes `const appliedRaw =
segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(…); applied = withoutResearchKeys(appliedRaw);`; restore's `const result =
contentVersionService.restoreVersion(…);` becomes `const restored = contentVersionService.restoreVersion(…); const
result = withoutResearchKeys(restored);`. Everything after each is unchanged; Task 24 reads `applied.before`,
`applyRaw.before`, `appliedRaw.before` and `restored.before`.

`propagationService.js`: `latestLiveEdit` runs the existing query selecting `id, edited_content, editor_id`;
`latestEditedText = (b, m, s) => { const row = latestLiveEdit(b, m, s); return row ? row.edited_content : null; }`. In
`createPropagatedEdits`, `const readRow = conn.prepare('SELECT * FROM segment_edits WHERE id = ?');` and `const
writes = [];`; per eligible occurrence, `const supersededAcceptanceIds = acceptanceService.supersedeForEdit(…, conn);`
(Task 15 made it return the ids); the in-place branch reads `const before = readRow.get(existingEdit.id);` before PR1's
`update.run(…)` and pushes `{ …, editId: existingEdit.id, inPlace: true, before, supersededAcceptanceIds }`; the
insert branch keeps `const info = insert.run(…)` and pushes `{ …, editId: Number(info.lastInsertRowid), inPlace:
false, before: null, supersededAcceptanceIds }`; both carry `chapter: occ.chapter`, `occurrence: occ.occurrence` and
PR1's `servedVersionId`. Return `{ created, skipped, writes }`.

The route: `const result = propagation.createPropagatedEdits(propagation.getDb(), { … });` becomes `const created =
propagation.createPropagatedEdits(propagation.getDb(), { … }); const result = withoutResearchKeys(created);`, and
`res.json(result)` is unchanged (Task 25 reads `created.writes`).
